## Racewind Passage

The Racewind Passage was the strait in the Sea of Swords that separated the islands of Thelve and Askalet from the Sword Coast mainland, west of the library fortress of Candlekeep.

Geography
The narrowest part of the Racewind Passage between the mainland and Thelve was only  miles across. This straight crossed with Skoond's Run, the strait that separated the isles of Thelve from the three islands to the south – Arthoum, Nairn's Tomb, and Askalet.

Description
The strait's name came from the harsh northern winds that blew south through the day, only calming at dusk and resting the gales at dawn. As the winds relaxed, thick, cold, and wet fogs rose up from the water and reminded clinging to the area until morning.

## Raerest

Raerest, or colloquially the Prow, was the largest and the westernmost of the three islands located west of Thelve and the fortress library of Candlekeep, inhabited by swamp lizardfolk. During the century that followed the Spellplague, its was a part of the so-called Cimarine Isles.

Description
Like all seven islands west of the library, Raerest consisted of mountainous and rocky outcroppings of fissured granite covered in temperate woodlands and shrubbery. Pools of collected rainwater in rock basins and freshwater springs could be found on the island. Additionally, Raerest had a notable freshwater swamp area. Raerest also served as a shield against sea winds and storms for the eastern islands and was nicknamed that as "the Prow."

Raerest was of almost equal size to Thelve with the exception of a  long outcropping on the southeastern side of the island. The longest diagonal distance across the island was almost  and excluding the outcropping – approximately .

The island's only area of good anchorages was on the eastern shores. Apart from that dozen sites, all other sides of the island were assaulted by powerful sea winds. A huge rock rose up above Raerest's center and curved at its western end. This mountain was known as the Shield, and it was the most prominent of many similar but smaller jutting rocks. The Shield also hid rich deposits of copper that could be seen sparkling from the surface. By the late 14 century DR, the rock bore signs of mining done in the past – several deep holes burrowed into it in various places.

There were three small freshwater lakes located parallel to the eastern border of the Shield, all fed by many springs. The lakes were connected together and ended in a large unnamed swamp. To the lakes' south, they drained into a calm bay – a natural harbor encircled by the fortress of Roaringcrest, that was ruined by an unleashed wild magic field up by the liches of the Twilight Three, and claimed by time, elements, and plantlife.

Geography
Unglur was located to the west of Thelve, with two sister islands between – Sklorn to the east and Unglur in the middle. Together Sklorn, Unglur, and Raerest were known as the Crab Reach, or simply the Reach among locals. Unglur and Raerest were separated by  of the sea at the shortest distance, and approximately  from a small sparsely-populated peninsula called Cape Raeth on the Cloak Bay.

Flora & Fauna
The isle's rocks were permanently whitened by the abundant guano of its thousands-strong seabird population. The birds were netted and consumed by the lizardfolk tribes that inhabited Raerest's unnamed swamp. The lizardfolk used the same nets to catch fish, and they farmed clams in the waterways that drained the swamp.

The riots of Roaringcrest were known to house such deadly creatures as automatons, crimson death, and darktentacles.

History
The origin of the island's name was lost to the ages. However, sages believed that Raerest and its other six sibling islands were all named after sailors, pirates, and settlers who claimed these lands in the long-forgotten past. The ruined pirate stronghold was rumored to hold unlooted treasured as of the late 14 century DR if pirates and sailors were to be believed.

## Rawleigh Gallaway

Rawleigh Gallaway was the individual who ran the Gallaway Trading Coaster and traveled through Lonelywood, in the early 14 century DR.

Description
In his young days, Rawleigh was a fair-skinned human man with dark brown hair.

History
In the, Rawleigh Gallaway ran caravans for his father's trading coaster and ended up being stuck in the town of Lonelywood when Bremen's Run became cut off by barbarian forces of chieftain Wylfdene. Subsequently, Rawleigh, along with other family members, took over Gallaway Trading Coaster after Rawleigh's father, Ilmus Gallaway, was unable to be in charge due to his age and love of Cormyrian brandy. In the, Rawleigh still ran the family business. As a "one last favor," Rawleigh requested his trusted adviser and employee, Jemeliah, to lend his expertise and sharp mind to Deirdre Gallaway in Targos, who strove to prove herself as a capable businesswoman and merchant.

## Rautheene

Rautheene was a human wizard from Oerth and an apprentice of Mordenkainen in the 14 century DR.

Description
Rautheene was slender with long black hair and considered stunningly beautiful. She was "half a head taller" than both Elminster and Mordenkainen, and Ed Greenwood described her laugh as "the merriest tinkling laughter [he'd] ever heard."

Personality
Rautheene was very praiseful of Mordenkainen, insisting that she would call him the greatest archmage of Oerth. She had a tendency to a sarcastic tongue, which tended to not play out well when amongst powerful archmages. She enjoyed many foods of Earth; one of her favorite meals was smoked salmon covered in praline'n'cream ice cream.

Relationships
Rautheene was the apprentice of Mordenkainen. She frequently accompanied him on trips to Earth, and took the place of Dalamar of Krynn in the meetings of the Wizards Three, meeting with Elminster in Ed Greenwood's house.

History
Not much is known of Rautheene's history, except for her apprenticeship to Mordenkainen. She traveled back and forth frequently to House Greenwood to meet with Mordenkainen and Elminster to discuss spells.

## Rasilith

Rasilith was the capital city of the ancient realm of Thaeravel, a major city of Netheril, and later a settlement of the returned Shadovar.

Description
After its abandonment and the coming of Anauroch, Rasilith was partially buried by sand dunes but still stood intact. It had thick gray stone walls, standing structures, and a central fortress ringed with alabaster towers. The central courtyard of the fortress had a deep well that connected to a series of underground caverns and chambers with rivers of cool, pure water, linked to the Buried Realms of the Underdark. These were inhabited by the phaerimms and their laerti slaves.

Geography
Rasilith was north of the Windy Plains and Araugul the Goblinmount, and after the coming of Anauroch it lay in the Quarter of Emptiness in the Sword. The Angardt city of Kismet was founded near it in the Flats to the west.

History
Rasilith was the capital of Thaeravel, a realm of sorcerers founded around -4300 DR. The later expansion of Netheril and their quest for arcane secrets led to them assaulting Thaeravel and Rasilith in -3392 DR, wiping out the sorcerers and conquering the city. Rasilith became an important southern garrison for Netheril, and arcanists flocked to it to examine and learn from the ancient runes left behind by the Thaeravelians. The studies of Rasilith faded in the Mythallar Era, and during the Shadowed Age it was abandoned and left to the phaerimms as Netheril collapsed.

After the fall of Netheril the area became known as the Quarter of Emptiness, an especially desolate and uninhabitable region of the Sword, and became confused with Tower Hlithal, a much more recent ruin of Anauria. The deep tunnels and caverns beneath it became inhabited by up to thousands of laertis ruled by phaerimms, who sent their servants to kill any who risked discovering their lair.

In the early 1300's DR, a Bedine tribe located the ruins and deemed it a good location to settle, thanks to its intact walls and pure water supply. The first night that they camped there, laertis climbed up from their underground caverns and slaughtered almost the entire tribe, with only a few children escaping on camels.

After the return of Thultanthar in 1372 DR and the following extermination of phaerimms and greening of Anauroch, Rasilith was once again inhabited, this time resettled with Bedine people. They became urbanized and were assimilated into Netherese culture.

## Rabruin's Tor

Rabruin's Tor was a small settlement in the Hullack Forest in the East Reaches of Cormyr.

Description
The community lay atop a prominent crag (the eponymous Tor), and consisted of the families of Estann Rabruin and his five sons. They cut wood and ranched from the Tor throughout the 15 century DR.

Geography
The Tor was just outside of the eastern edge of the Hullack Forest along the East Way, on the southern side of the highway.

History
In the early 1330s DR, the woodcutter and wainwright Estann Rabruin bought and settled the Tor, building his family's home there. As part of his ambitions, he also backed a project to cut a road through the Hullack Forest called the Hullackheart Trail that would run from Thunderstone to meet the East Way below the Tor. This project was quickly noticed by the Crown of Cormyr, who realized it would disturb the monsters and ruins in the depths of the forest, and create a new, remote settlement that would easily foment problems for the realm while demanding an official Purple Dragons garrison, a King's lord, and other administrative necessities. Unwilling to pay the price so that some merchants could profit off of it, King Rhigaerd Obarskyr II decreed a ban on all woodcutting within a half-day's travel of the north end of Stag's Skull Bridge, effectively ending the effort.

Although the roadbuilding project ended, Estann Rabuin stubbornly remained on the Tor with his family, who continued to woodcut and ranch from their elevated homestead.

## Ravaer's Stronghold

Ravaer's Stronghold was a simple earthworks fort east of Thunderstone in Cormyr.

Location
The fort lay in the rolling plains east of Thunderstone, just below the foothills of the Thunder Peaks near High Dale. It was surrounded by open grasslands used by ranchers for grazing cattle.

Structure
The fort was made from a simple earthen ring wall surrounded by a ditch. An elevated earth causeway just wide enough to fit two riders or a small wagon approached it from the west with ditches on either side, with a sharp dogleg turn to make approaching the entry even more complicated.

A stone-built chamber beneath the earthworks provided a final holdout and shelter for the garrison. It was about  square, with niches in the walls for sleeping benches. Three stone caskets resembling sarcophagi sat in the middle of the room, that the garrison used as tables, as well as securely storing skins of water, raw cabbages, clay-sealed sausages, and firewood. If necessary, they could also be used as coffins.

The refuge chamber was accessed by a long, narrow ramp that was normally sealed by a stone slab on the surface. The slab lay in the floor of a small earthwork cave on the surface of the fort that had hitching rings for sheltering horses.

History
The fort was named for a Purple Dragon commander called Ravaer, who died on the spot fighting off a raiding force sponsored by Sembia. The fort was later built to repel small invasions coming from the Thunder Peaks to the west.

## Rapzin Ellestyn

History
At some point, before her exile from the Night Masks and the city of Westgate, Rinara, the Lady of Shivs, had some dealings with Rapzin Ellestyn, and upon her return, warned her companions to watch their purses around the drow merchant. In turn, Rapzin blamed that warning on Rinara's prejudice. Their past dealing was in smuggling and selling certain difficult-to-move items for a well-known criminal organization, presumably the infamous Night Masks.

In the, Rinara and her companions had to do business with the drow once again in order to acquire his anti-psion magic stone that Rapzin used in dealing with creatures that could read and affect his mind. The stone would later be used against the so-called Master of Undergate, psion Kajeel.

## Rade

Rade was a werewolf servant of Selûne, who was part of the New Moon Pact in the 8 century DR.

Description
As a werewolf, Rade appeared as a bulky black wolf.

History
Rade served Selûne as a werewolf of the New Moon Pact, fighting against the faith's enemies with exceptional cunning and violence. In 757 DR, the High Moonmistress Brima Chintamn of Bright Lady's Tower in Chancelgaunt declared the New Moon Pact a heresy, and Rade and many other members were hunted down, with Rade killed before he could be captured.

When Feena Archwood investigated the mystery of the New Moon Pact in 1373 DR, Rade's spirit appeared in her dreams alongside the other members of the Pact to defend and guide her. After she uncovered Velsinore's heresy and they mutually slew each other, Feena's spirit was met by a Shard escort to the afterlife, but Rade and the other Pact members encouraged her to return to life and join the New Moon Pact herself. With the aid of Julith Harkspur's healing, Feena was revived and ultimately continued the legacy of the Pact.

## Rain catcher

A rain catcher was a simple contraption used to collect rainwater in the wilderness. They were considered an important tool for adventurers journeying into regions where sources of freshwater were unsafe to drink, such as in the jungles of Chult.

Description
The purpose of a rain catcher was to serve as a lightweight and mobile basin for catching rainwater. It comprised a  tarp made of leather that was stretched across a wooden frame that could easily be folded for transport. When erected, it could collect rain water at a rate of about  per  of rain, and it could hold up to .

History
As of the late 15 century DR, explorers in Chult were advised to use rain catchers or to boil any river water in the jungles to avoid contracting throat leeches. The sale of rain catchers was strictly regulated in Port Nyanzaru—the only city in Chult at the time—with all sales being controlled by the merchant prince Ekene-Afa. They could be bought at the city's Red Bazaar for one gold piece.

## Rax

Rax was a powerful shadow mastiff summoned by Variance Amatick in 1373 DR to attack the people of Yhaunn.

Description
Rax was exceptionally large and powerful among shadow mastiffs, the size of a warhorse and covered in scars.

History
On the night of the new moon on Eleasis 15, in the, the shade priestess of Shar Variance Amatick summoned 18 shadow mastiffs and then pulled forth Rax. Invoking ancient contracts, she offered Rax command of half of the mastiffs and the mission of hunting and killing in the Stiltways while she attacked Moonshadow Hall and her unwilling servant Keph Thingoleir stole The Leaves of One Night from Fourstaves House. In exchange, the lordly mastiff would be allowed to hunt at will in Faerûn for three nights.

Rax agreed to the terms and took the other shadow mastiffs on a hunt in the Stiltways, distracting the city guard and preventing them from defending Moonshadow hall when it too was attacked. Fortunately for the people of the Stiltways, the residents of Fourstaves House—the spellcasters Strasus, Dagnalla, Roderio, Malia and Krin—burst forth to defend the city and managed to prevent the shadow mastiffs from killing anyone. Realizing the threat the great beast and its pack posed, Feena Archwood and Keph forced Variance to banish them early, returning them to their home plane.

## Rautym

Rautym were a species of anomalous psionic monkey-like creatures found in the caverns and underground locations of the Savage Frontier in the North Faerûn.

Description
Rautym were small  tall monkey-like creatures. They were completely hairless with their backs freckled with sports, and they had no eyes.

Behavior
Rautym were pack animals and surely where there was one rautym, the rest of the pack were not far. The creatures stuck together, not just because of being weak or fearful, but because, as a group, a pack of rautym gained power from their kin.

Their lairs were filled with small, faintly phosphorescent stones, and rautym carried such rocks when traveling away from their lairs. The rocks served as psionic beacons and aided rautym's navigation. As such, they were vulnerable to spells that created magical darkness and silence. When faced with these spells, rautym fled in panic.

When a pack of rautym participated in evoking dance magic, they often ensured their privacy by stationing shadow-formed members of the packs as sentries. Interrupted rautym dance always ensured rage and destruction.

Abilities

They possessed psionic abilities to feel sound and hear light that served as a powerful substitute to eyesight, made even more effective through danger sense. Rautym also could affect their bodies through psychometabolism: they could take on a shadow-form and use such devotions as adrenalin control, aging, and chameleon power.

Their unique ability was a form of dance magic that allowed a group of rautym to work magic through a specific form of dance. When at least two rautym are together, they could utilize this strange ability, with one of the creatures dancing and the other – as a spell focus. A single-dancer spell-casting was time-consuming – the simplest first level of complexity spells could take a single dancer rautym five hours to cast. But with more dancing rautym, they were able to cast much more powerful spells. Any spell could be cast this way, as long as the focus rautym knew of the spell or witnessed it being cast in the past. Roughly speaking, the number of dancers needed to be doubled per spell complexity level, and it was not unheard of for a huge group of rautym to spend days accumulating magic enough to unleash unknown alien spells of astonishing power.

Combat
Rautym used their psionic abilities in combat extensively. The first thing they did when faced with danger was use the adrenalin control power to boost their physical strength dramatically. When using stealth against their enemies, rautym used their shadow-form psionic ability. From the depth of shadows, rautym affected their enemies with aging, or simply surprise-attacked.

Ecology
Rautym did not need eyesight, and they lived in caverns and other underground habitats. A single pack of rautym could count from as few as four creatures or as many as forty rautym. All rautym in a pack were led by an elder, who also acted as the focus in the dance magic rituals. A pack could have multiple elders, as many as in a one-to-four ratio.

Before they could lay siege to Esmeltaran and claim the Armory of Nedeheim, Rashturl raised an army and led it against the invaders, defeating them and sending the Horned Mage into hiding.

Rasa was the daughter of King Dhanar Parhek, the twin of Prince Dhanar the Younger, and the elder sister of Prince Malek the Coinmage. She married Dornar Steelsar, and they had their children Choris, Rosa, Rohav, and Rhothar.

History
Rasa was moved as a child to Silverymoon after her father was killed and Amn fell into civil war. Living under the protection of Lady Alustriel Silverhand, she married Dornar Steelsar and had four children together. They were eventually found by Amnian assassins, however, and in the, she was forced to flee the city with Rhothar while Dornar and Choris fought to the death, and Rohav was kidnapped. ## Randalf

Randalf was a lightfoot halfling from the Rivington district of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Randalf was a handsome, polite, and well-dressed pale-skinned blond halfling with blue eyes. He wore a quality leather jerkin, a leather circlet, and simple muddy-green breeches.

History
In the, the army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees in the process along the way. However, Sylvie Summerworth was not particularly frightened by the encroaching army. Instead of being worried about the looming doom to the city of Baldur's Gate, Randalf and his friend Sylvie Summerworth stressed over undelivered pigeon mail. Randalf attended to Sylvie as she raged over Sword Coast Courier pigeon mail and suggested others to hold on to the mail until the situation was resolved. Unbeknownst to most, the pigeons were hunted and eaten by Gale Dekarios's tressym named Tara, who recently followed her master to Baldur's Gate from Waterdeep.

## Ralamnish Ridings

The Ralamnish Ridings was a distant frontier region of Calimshan during its Third Age.

Geography
The Ridings broadly consisted of the northernmost territories of ancient Calimshan in the area that would later be known as Amn, though the extents varied greatly with time.

History
Empire of Calimshan
The Third Age of Calimshan ( to ) saw the conquest and settlement of the lands north of the imperial core, of which the lands of Tethyr, Mir, and Iltkazar became known as the Calishar Emirates. A long-running war with the elves of the Forest of Tethir ( to ) continually sapped the strength of Calimshan, but expeditionary armies pushed beyond the Forest of Tethir regardless.

The initial effort to conquer the Ralamnish Ridings was overseen by the scout commander Ralamn, who led the army and organized the construction of numerous outposts in the lands between the Tejarn Hills, Troll Mountains, and Lake Esmel. The conquest of a great kingdom of trolls further north led to the naming of Trollford and the Troll Mountains, and the near-extinction of trolls in the region.

Despite efforts of the empire, elven and goblin raiders perpetually troubled the Ridings, and the extreme distance from Calimshan presented many difficulties. The beholder invasion from the Alimir Mountains around  caused a reduction of troops on northern frontier as resources were drawn away to deal with the critical threat they posed. Although the garrisons were weakened, Calimshan maintained overall control of the Ridings from that time onwards.

Shoon Imperium
During the Shoon Imperium, a number of cities were founded in the adjacent Western Emirate of Amin, including Athkatla (100 DR), Crimmor (163 DR), Murann (212 DR), and Trademeet (223 DR), providing the basis for later urban civilization. The imperial advisor Saref Adnan had a villa in the Ridings, and he lived out his last days there in madness after gaining the enmity of every faith and priest, and perished of a curable illness in 111 DR.

Before his brief rule as Qysar, Amahl Shoon VI commanded and fought alongside the army in the Ridings. His brother Shoon IV went on to rule from 205 DR to 281 DR, and decided that a light hand over the Ridings was preferable to forcible colonization. He allowed small, autonomous realms to form and develop in their own way so that they could be exploited later, with 227 DR seeing Meiritin founded around the Tejarn Hills and Tathtar along the Deepwash. During this time, the Mad Mage Ilhundyl rampaged about the region from 224 DR till 238 DR, when he was killed by Elminster and Myrjala. The city of Xandar was built in 250 DR, but only lasted ten years before being destroyed by a volcanic eruption.

Legacy
The name of the Ralamnish Ridings eventually faded from use, replaced by the realms of Meiritin, Tathtar, Minsorran, Valashar, Cortryn, and eventually Amn. The surviving few fortifications became known as the Nine Sentinels, while the lengthy road built to connect the disparate garrisons and allow the speedy march of armies from the south remained known as the South Road.

## Ralgorax

Ralgorax, known as the "Sword of Tyr", was a mighty paladin who lived sometime before the mid–14 century DR.

Possessions
He fought with a great sword and shield.

History
Ralgorax died in battle alongside his horse, yet many years later—when both were little more than shriveled corpses—they rode again as undead to defend Ralgorax's home village from a nighttime attack by a horde of orcs. He rode through the village while banging his sword and shield to rouse and warn the people, and then fought off the orcish scouts until dawn. But in the light of day, the villagers were disgusted to see his undead appearance, so he sadly saluted them before charging to meet the horde head-on.

## Rakasta

Rakasta  were a race of intelligent catlike humanoids, non-magical cousins of rakshasas, native to the world of Mystara and found inhabiting the Vast on Toril.

Description
Rakasta were humanoids with feline features who walked on their legs in a manner similar to that of humans but showing grace and fluidity in their movements. Their bodies were covered in soft fur of orange-brown, light tan, or dark brown colors. They had feline heads and bright green feline eyes with vertical pupils. They had furry nonprehensile tails from  to  long. Rakasta heights carried from  to .

Apart from the most common rakasta breed, called a "basic rakasta" or "moggie," there existed a multitude of rakasta breeds found in different parts of Mystara and different worlds or planes. Each breed had its own distinct feline appearance, size, physical and mental traits, and various levels of fear of water. Generally, across all breeds, males were larger than females.

Personality

Rakasta were proud nomads and barbarians, many of whom were peaceful warriors who retaliated swiftly if provoked.

All rakasta breeds were also great at hiding and concealing themselves, even the larger breeds. Just like domestic cats, rakasta were affected by catnip. The plant was hard to resist, prompting rakasta to compulsively roll around in catnip and rub it all over in a state of ecstasy.

Abilities
All rakasta breeds that belonged to the "greater rakasta" group had the ability to emit a great terrifying roar that could affect their opponents. Another notable ability of all rakasta breeds was granted to them by their feline physiology. All rakasta except for the basic rakasta but "moggies," was their feline physiology that allowed them to tense their muscles like a fine spring, releasing it in a burst of strength. In order to have energy for such feats, rakasta had to sleep for 12 hours each day and supplement it with regular catnaps. Being deprived of the long sleep left them unable to focus such bursts of strength and could result in physical weakness, and dimmed mental acuity. Non-adventuring rakasta normally slept 16 hours a day. Sleeping rakasta were not defenseless. They had a highly sensitive sleep that kept them alert to even the most muffled suspicious sound. They were hard to surprise during rest, and rakasta were not disturbed by snoring companions, howling wind, or any other type of white noise.

Rakasta whiskers were an important sensory organ that granted them vibrissae, a natural boost to dexterity through air-current detection. If their whiskers were damaged, it affected rakasta's nimbleness and agility until the whiskers grew back within one to four weeks.

 Combat

When provoked, rakasta were fearsome warriors rarely using normal weapons. They needed no tools when rakasta claws and bites were as deadly as any blade, especially with the claw argumentations coming among these felines. Rakasta wore kasas – a special type of metallic clawed gauntlets. Their back claws were just as dangerous. If a rakasta warrior landed two slashes on a target, they could also land a lightning-fast rending with their rear claws.

The closest alternative to a cavalry among rakasta were hatra – warriors who rode saber-toothed tigers into battle. Hatra were considered to be the bravest and the strongest of the pride as only the most valiant rakasta could commend saber-toothed tigers' respect. Hatra's saber-toothed tigers were equipped with a special variety of saddles designed especially for the cats' leaping abilities. These saddles kept hatra on the mount's back without a chance of falling off and giving them the ability to use both hands in battle while on the tigerback.

Rakasta were also known to keep magical creatures called ebon tigers, using them as guards.

Society
Rakasta were nomadic people who lived in prides of between six and 60 adults with an additional 25% of the total number of adults being rakasta young, led by a single chief whose word is the absolute law. Additionally, a single rakasta pride could keep up to 12 saber-toothed tigers. Six hatra always accompanied a pride's chief, keeping them safe. Each pride had at least one cleric and, on occasion, a mage. When not moving, the felines erected temporary tent cities of many colorful pavilions.

The colorfulness of rakasta tent cities was the evidence of their artistry and admirable skills at weaving rugs, silks, tapestries, and masterfully crafted and decorated bowls, cups, and other receptacles. Rakasta valued such items of great quality and esthetics more than gold and gems, making their treasuries filled with objects of art and products of master-crafters.

Rakasta society valued warriors' prowess and kept their hatra at the top of the rakasta society. Apart from the chief's words, rakasta followed the Sri'raka, the code of conduct. Some of the tenets dictated by Sri'raka included the laws of no combat challenge ever to be refused; no wounded to be ever left behind, warriors were to transport the wounded to safety or grant them honorable death; all rakasta strove to die in battle and never in their sleep; no mere would be given to the enemies, and no mercy was to be expected in return; the only time rakasta were allowed to retreat – under orders to regroup and attack again within two sunrises; and lastly, rakasta never surrendered, those who were taken prisoners were not rakasta anymore.

Rakasta had their own language that carried in dialects depending on the pride's culture. More primitive rakasta prides learned to speak Common with a thick purring feline accent of rolling r's and hissed s's.

Ecology

In the lands populated by the rakasta, the feline hunters served to control the game herd populations. Those who allied with rakasta earned themselves reliable trading partners.

History
The history of rakasta on Toril remained unrecorded; however, the rakasta creation myth on the World of Mystara was quite ancient. It was a poem about a clever cat named Ba-steh who fell in love with her human master – Kum-rah, a shaman who worshipped The Immortal Ka. The cat protected her master and served Ka. Eventually, the deity-like Immortal rewarded the cat with a transformation into a human woman. Ba-steh married Kum-rah. Subsequently, Ka tested the couple by sending out a mouse to Kum-rah's cave shrine. Ba-steh followed her instincts and pounced on the rodent, killing it. The shaman witnessed his wife's animalistic behavior in dismay. Ka was upset with the test's outcome. The Immortal wanted to transform Ba-steh back into a cat but realized that it would be cruel, and instead, he transformed both lovers into cat-folk, the first rakasta.

After Kum-rah's ninth death, Ba-steh was given a place by Ka's side to distract her from pain and grief. She learned to use magic, discovered the Outer Planes, and eventually left Ka behind on a quest for knowledge across the universe and achieved immortality. By the time Ba-steh left Mystara, she had already had countless children. As a final gift to her, Ka transformed the most loyal cat followers of his into rakasta, effectively creating a new race that spread to a Mystaran moon – Patera, which became the largest rakasta empire in all the stars. According to the legends, Ba-steh traveled across the planes and time and eventually found a world where she could be worshipped as a deity, becoming Bastet. Centuries passed, and the civilization that worshipped Bastet collapsed, sending the immortal feline back to Mystara, where she found her rakasta flourishing, becoming the main Immortal patron of the rakasta people.

By the late 14 century DR, a group of hatra from a Vastar rakasta tribe found themselves owing much to certain unnamed rakshasa Rajahs. In turn, the Rajahs owed favors to the individual only known as the Sewer Lich, lending the undead service of the indebted hatra. The Sewer Lich used a duo of rakshasas – Blevins and Abdulthe, and the rakasta as its assassins.

## Ravens Bluff Trumpeter

The Ravens Bluff Trumpeter, or simply The Trumpeter, was a monthly periodical broadsheet that reported on a wide variety of topics, from election coverage and war updates to preposterous scandals and opinion pieces, and even classifieds.

Contents
Each issue had several set columns run by dedicated editors. The Editor-in-Chief Fred Faber often took charge of hot news and general direction of the broadsheet, Clio Hesperin was the editor who curated and wrote the City News section, and the Society Editor, "Jackie" Jacinth Moonspring, ran the section dedicated to celebrities, nobility, and other individuals of note and their proclivities, social announcements, and gossips. Only these three columns were regular content. Much other coverage consisted of announcements, advertisements, classifieds, public letters, and other miscellaneous articles.

Availability
Ravens Bluff Trumpeter was often distributed via street hawker children who sold the latest issues for two copper pieces, with a customary tip of 50 gp among the adventuring population. Some establishment, such as the Sleepy Dwarf Tavern, posted the latest issues of Ravens Bluff Trumpeter on their notice boards to all to read.

Staff

Editorial
Fred Faber, the Editor-in-Chief in the late 14 century DR.
Strunk Norbert, the Editor-in-Chief predecessor of master Faber.
Cuthbert Ironbeard III, an editorial contributor during Strunk Norbert's, the Editor-in-Chief's run.
Clio Hesperin, the reporter who run the City News column in the late 14 century DR.
Damien Blackthorn, a ninth shift editor in the late 14 century DR.
Guido, a poorly-educated dockside reporter in the late 14 century DR.
Harold Dayspring, one of the co-owners of the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter, killed by a runaway chariot circa 1372 DR.
Jacinth Moonspring, the editor and reporter who handled the Society section of the broadsheet in the late 14 century DR.
Whitney Wordsmlth, the managing editor, who worked alongside Strunk Norbert, the Editor-in-Chief.
Silva Sinaran, a highborn society reporter from the late 14 century DR.

Reporters and Contributors
Al Kadeem, an occasional contributor who covered topics associated with the Temple of Tyr in the late 14 century DR.
Alisuies Relthana, a Lady Knight of the Mystic Flame and an occasional contributor who covered the unification of the Ministry of Art and the Wizard's Guild in the late 14 century DR.
Amber Lynn Thoden, ironically enough, the alter-ego of the infamous warlord and enemy of Ravens Bluff and one of the regular contributors.
Armond Arhesian, the reporter who penned articles on Ambassador Belakira of Thay in the late 14 century DR.
Borrek Halloway, a journalist who was murdered in the late 14 century DR.
Dilyard Noonbar, a journalist who penned New Threat to City Says Wizard's Guild in the late 14 century DR.
Gandalf Wishspoon, a journalist killed in a gang war of Crow's End in 1370 DR.
Harold Dayspring, one of the Tumpeter's reporters who penned articles on breaking news, such as Brazen Attack Against Lord Blacktree in the late 14 century DR.
Jonah Mindspring, the reporter who held an interview with the Senior Harper in Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.
Nadd Parker, a freelance reporter in the late 14 century DR.
Rupert T. Hangman, a judge who penned conviction announcements for The Trumpeter in the late 14 century DR.
Silva Sinaran, a manorborn society reporter and Lord Imbrel's youngest sister in the late 14 century DR.
Symore Ceasmore, a reporter who covered major announcements from the Ministry of Art in the late 14 century DR.
Tara Strongquill, a dockside reporter who was abducted by the city's criminal elements in the late 14 century DR.
Terrance Heldersporn, a reporter who penned articles about the Temple of Selûne in Ravens Bluff.
Toby Wartwarden, a journalist who penned the Bandits of the Red Sun Raid Ravens Bluff Caravans article in the late 14 century DR.
Tomaldi Everspring, a late 14 century DR employee of the Trumpeter and a contributor who wrote about religious organizations.
Victoria Morales, a sea captain and an occasional contributor in the late 14 century DR.

Other Staff
Myra Miller, one of the clerks in the broadsheet's employ in the late 14 century DR.

History
In Ches of the, a Trumpeter clerk – Myra Miller, received a mention in that month's issue of Ravens Bluff Trumpeter. After the offices of the gazette were ransacked and torched (evidently by Talassans) while the staff was away at the local printer's shop, Myra showed quick thinking and managed to save the organization's documents from being destroyed. Despite Myra Miller's best efforts, all reports of the summer meteor crisis of the  were lost.

In the, one of the casualties in the recent gang war was Borrek Halloway, a journalist who worked for The Trumpeter's ninth shift editor Damien Blackthorn. Borrek was just the latest among a series of grizzly killings, and a group of the Living City adventurers answered a summoning from the editor and accepted his contract to uncover the truth behind the victim's death. Borrek's killing could've been the result of his work on such investigative pieces as Corruption in the City Government, Bribery in the City Watch, Merchant's Guild Inflating Prices, and Organized Criminal Guilds: Parasites of Society.

In the, The Trumpeter's best investigative reporter, Tara Strongquill, fell victim to a kidnapping by the notorious chaos agent named Locaath Blackmanta. The kidnapping was a stunt aimed to sew panic in Ravens Bluff. The Ravens Bluff Trumpeter hired a band of adventurers to find the culprit and rescue the unlucky supporter. The reporter was eventually rescued, to Fred Faber's relief, but the villain remained at large.

Rumors & Legends
In the late 14 century DR, Bellona Helmguard of Bellona's Baths claimed that, according to the brother of a man who worked at the Beholder's Eye, the editor-in-chief of The Trumpeter was a vampire, explaining his night shift work.

## Ravens Bluff Sanitation Facility

The Ravens Bluff Sanitation Facility was a facility that destroyed and recycled garbage in the city of Ravens Bluff. It was established sometime in the 14 century DR under decree by Mayor Charles Oliver O'Kane, replacing a junkyard.

Location
This facility sat on the east side of Zeldazar Street, in the Pumpside neighborhood of Ravens Bluff's Southside District.

Structure

The Sanitation Facility was surrounded by a  high stone fence, covered in climbing flowers and ivy. It had single gate, pair of iron-reinforced wooden doors. A sign written in common hung over the gates that read "Ravens Bluff Sanitation Facility" in Common, followed beneath by the words, "We pay for your TRASH".

Infront of the gate stood a gate house. Northwest of it stood a small residential building. And in the central back of the facility was the "Storage Facility," which had an above-ground and one below-ground floor, with a garbage chute on the first floor that dropped trash down onto the facility's magical waste destroyer.

Several small incense burners could be found lit around the corners of the facility, elliminating foul odors from its trash and warding of various vermin that had a tendency to visit the dump. Two small water towers also stood within the facility, used to put out the infrequent fires that occurred from stockpiles of flammable garbage like paper and the foul odors that such fires produced.

Services
By law the citizens of Ravens Bluff had to collect and dispose of their waste at this facility, but to encourage the populace to follow the law the city paid one copper piece per  of trash brought in. Tanneries and other businesses that produced hazardous garbaged were given a higher reward of three copper pieces per  of trash they disposed of properly.

All trash was weighed upon a large, plain-looking iron scale that stood next to the gatehouse. It stood  tall, measured  wide, and weighed . Sometimes people tried to cheat the scales by "loading" their trash with rocks or weights, thus the facility's workers made sure to check all trash during the weighing process. Anyone caught trying to cheat the scales would be fined an amount of copper pieces that was equal to the loaded weight of their trash multiplied by 10.

The city's reward for trash disposal encouraged a lot of participation from vagrants and adventurers who were down-on-their luck. The trash was also delivered to the dump by a group of roguish street cleaners employed by the city. Once in the hands of the facility garbage was seperated into glass, metal, paper, wood, decomposing food, weeds, spell components, and anything else that could be melted down or sold. There existed an unofficial rule amongst the facility's workers that anything valuable could be kept by the worker who found it.

Once per week the stockpiled trash was put up for sale to Ravenians and people from the surrounding countryside, with the exception of dangerous materials like acids and poisons. Farmers typically bought up compostable garbage for use as fertilizer or feed. All wood scraps were carted off to the city's harbor and the neighborhood of Torchtown.
Waste Disposal
All trash that was not sold by the end of a week was disposed of by placing it upon a sphere of annihilation. This sphere was kept secured within a vault made of cement and stone, with thick lead panels bolted onto its interior walls, and an iron door that was  thick. There was only one key to the vault, kept in the hands of the facility's resident wizard, and if anyone other than that wizard tried to move the sphere a magic mouth would activate, shouting the message, "Alert the City Watch. Someone is trying to steal the sphere!". As an extra precaution, the room that the vault was kept within had a glyph of warding, mimicking the effects of flame strike, that was cast by a powerful priest on the floor between the entrance and the vault.

Originally, the existence of the sphere of annihilation was known by only the facility's chief staff and the city's mayor, with the wider public believing excess trash was simply dumped into a dry well. Over time word of the sphere's existence became widely whispered, making it a common occurrence for children to come around asking to see it. As a security precaution, anyone who wished to do view the sphere had to wear a "restraining suit" that kept them chained to the wall.

Inhabitants
The facility was chiefly operated by John Porter, its sole foreman, and its resident wizard Marian Wicksal, who provided its sphere of annihilation from among the trove of magic items she had gained back when she was an adventurer. She lived at the dump alongside her pet pseudodragon, Rouge, and had weekly first picks of any spell components.

During daylight hours Porter was usually stood inside the gatehouse, drinking coffee. He oversaw a dozen untrained human laborers who had escaped from slavery somewhere across the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Various small vermin had frequented the dump, including gulls, rats, ravens, and squirrels. These were often warded off by the incense that was lit around the facility, though larger animals like rogue bears were not put off. Occasionally outside help was hired to chase away larger animals or to hunt them down.

## Ratha Rann

Ratha Rann the Eagle-Eyed was one of the retired adventurers inhabiting Nellie Thursday Home for Experienced Adventurers sometime in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Ratha was an aging man of . He had a  frame and wore optic aids to help his near-sighted brown eyes. He dyed his gray hair black. Ratha adopted a womanizing habit of chasing every skirt that crossed his eyes to recapture his lost youth. The adventurer Ada the Unbending was one of the targets of Ratha's affections.

Possessions
The man's adventuring gear included an enchanted +2 chain mail, a magic +1 shield, a deadly +1 longsword, a +2 longbow, and enchanted +1 arrows. However, his most notable possession was a pair of the bifocals of the eagle.

Abilities
Ratha spoke several languages including the Common, Luiric, Elvish, and Hill giant languages. He was trained in fighting with axes, spears, longswords, and knives, and was a capable marksman with javelins and longbows. As an adventurer, Rann was a proficient blind fighter, knew survival skills such as fire-building, and had a reliable direction sense and tracking. He also knew how to play a lyre.

As a ranger, Rann could cast some limited magics from the plant and animal spheres.

History
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Ratha Rann was 92 and had been retired for around 25 years, following some unexpected financial reverses. In his retirement years, Ratha Rann desperately tried to prove to everyone and himself that he was not yet old, showing it by courting every attractive female he met. One of his conquests was a woman who was 22 years younger than the retired adventurer, but when they planned to move in together, the age difference ended up being a public scandal and caused a breakup, leaving the man single ever since.

However, Ada the Unbending mesmerized the aging adventurer and, to impress her, Ratha Rann embarked on an adventure that ended up dragging him across several worlds, including Olympus, where they met the deity Hermes; the jungles of Quorsit; and the demi-plane of Kansas. He would face a frightful frost giant called Icehand in a duel.

## Rain linnorm

Rain linnorms were a species of linnorm, a group of very rare, primitive but powerful dragons.

Description
Rain linnorms were massive, serpentine creatures that ranged from  long plus a -long tail at hatching, to  plus a -long tail as great wyrms. They had teardrop-shaped scales which were shiny white on hatchlings, and which grew thicker and harder as they aged and which, on adults, could change colors between white, grey, blue, or green at the linnorm's will.

Personality
Like other linnorms, rain linnorms were cruel and destructive. They were also incredibly vain: they took-nay, demanded- credit for the heinous actions they committed. They were also greedy for treasure.

Abilities
Like all types of dragons, rain linnorms possessed potent magical abilities as well as a nasty breath weapon: a stream of boiling water some  wide and  long. They were inherently immune to damage caused by electricity; old rain linnorms were additionally invulnerable to missile weapons, while very old individuals were also immune to nonmagical blunt weapons, and those old enough to be called venerable were immune to nonmagical edged weapons. The rare wyrms inherently regenerated. They also gained the following inherent spell-like abilities as they aged:
Very young: create food and water twice per day.
Young: entangle and plant growth each thrice per day.
Juvenile: call lightning twice per day.
Young adult: lightning bolt twice per day and water breathing at will.
Adult: control winds twice per day.
Mature adult: moonbeam and rainbow each thrice per day.
Old: transmute dust to water thrice per day.
Very old: weather summoning twice per day.
Venerable: conjure water elemental twice per day.
Wyrm: control weather once per day.
Great wyrm: wind walk once per day.

Combat

Immature rain linnorms were impetuous and quick to attack at the chance of treasure. They typically started with their breath weapon and magical abilities, but would claw and bite an victims they perceived as nonthreatening, so as to avoid damaging potential treasure.

Mature rain linnorms, however, disdained physical combat and relied solely on their far more potent magical abilities before resorting to their breath weapon.

Ecology
Rain linnorms made their lairs deep within hills, hiding their treasure within the dark burrows. But they rested within only when the weather was pleasant, preferring to sleep in open and be buffeted by wind and rain. Rain linnorms attempted to kill any intelligent creature that approached their lair. And if one suspected that the location of it's lair was known, it would find a new lair elsewhere and move every single piece of treasure.

Rain linnorms were a species of incredibly solitary individuals, believing as they did that all other creatures, up to and including other rain linnorms, were beneath them. The only exception was during breeding season, when mated pairs watched over their nests. These pairs left each other and the nest as soon as the eggs hatched.

It was said that while rain linnorms could eat anything, their favorite food was lightning bolts. They had no natural predators except for foolish adventurers.

## Ravaerris

Ravaerris was a Zhentarim stronghold and outpost constructed on the edge of Anauroch. By the late 15 century DR, it was a buried and long-abandoned ruin said to contain many unclaimed magical treasures.

Description
Ravaerris was a large fort established as a staging point for Zhentarim prospectors tasked with discovering and excavating treasures of ancient Netheril. It was notable for its many special vaults designed to house the artifacts they unearthed. Following the events of the Spellplague, the entire fort was swallowed by the ground, but it was rumored to have remained intact.

Geography
The stronghold was located on the eastern edge of Anauroch, west of the Border Forest. By the, the forest had seemingly expanded to include the buried fort's former location.

History
The fort was established in the 14 century DR sometime before the Return of Netheril. As of the time of its destruction, Ravaerris was the largest of the Zhentarim's outposts along the edge of Anauroch. Despite many challenges and hardships, the Zhents stationed there managed to accrue an impressive trove of ancient Netherese treasures procured from the dangerous desert, potentially including relics, spells, captive creatures, and magic potent enough to slay the Princes of Shade.

Ravaerris remained under Zhentarim control until the events of the Spellplague in the, during which some unknown artifact or magic housed within the vaults began to emit waves of force that caused a hidden fault to appear beneath the fort. This fault swallowed Ravaerris whole and then sealed itself shut, burying the fort below ground. The few Zhentarim survivors were convinced that the fort had endured this ordeal, and for the next century, members of the faction maintained the belief that Ravaerris's treasures remained waiting to be reclaimed.

Inhabitants
Prior to the Spellplague, the fort housed a whole company of Zhentarim prospectors seeking Netherese relics, aided by diggers, diviners, and soldiers.

He was rumored to be the master of the thieves' guild in the Loft, but it was actually controlled by his brother, Silvin Andarlis.

## Ramship

Ramships were small, simple galleys used by the pirates of Immurk's Hold to defend their harbor against unwanted intruders.

Description
Ramships were very small at only  long (more like a launch or rowboat in scale than a proper galley), and were crewed by ten rowers (prisoners or slaves) and piloted by a loyal pirate. They were cheaply built and very fragile, and not necessarily expected to survive their ramming attacks. They could also be loaded up with flammables and set alight for extra effect against dire foes, though this ensured that the ramship would definitely be lost (the crew were expected to swim back).

History
In the 14 century DR, ten ramships were kept in a sea cave near the entrance of the Immurk's Hold harbor. Two were active at any moment, and monitored visiting ships that approached the entrance. In the event that ships attempted to attack the harbor—and managed to overcome the malenti, harbor chain, and artificial reefs—all ten ramships were put into action. The ramships served to weaken and slow the invaders while the overlooking Citadel used its Thayvian bombards and the ships in the harbor prepared to respond.

## Rapier of the High Road

Rapiers of the High Road were enchanted rapiers created especially for larcenous purposes.

Powers
These rapiers possessed a +1 enchantment that made the blade more deadly and its aim more precise. Its keen blade could land devastating and deadly slashes and it produced acid that dealt additional damage with each landed attack.

History
The origin of the rapiers of the High Road was unknown, however, these blades' light weight and ease of concealment made them a favorite weapon of bandits who preyed on fat merchants and vulnerable travelers. One thing was known for sure – these rapiers were not created for noble purposes as whoever held such weapon received a larcenous gleam in their eyes with the weapon filling their minds with rich purchases and tempted the wielders to take the coin from others.

As of the, at least one rapier of the High Road found its way into the mega-dungeon of Undermountain underneath the city of Waterdeep. In the, one such blade could be found in the city of Neverwinter, sold by a scoundrel merchant Fibba in the Docks District.

## Radija

Radija was a Mistress of Souls, a necromancer of the Absolute in the Moonrise Towers during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Radija wore the cult's dark-red colored uniform robes with the holy symbol of the Absolute adorning her chest and an enchanted Myrkulite scourge at her side. This human woman had dark brown skin and long curly brown hair. Her face was decorated with a painted design around her eyes.

History
In or shortly before the, Radija joined the ranks of the Absolutists. However, she never became the True Soul. Like other necromancers of the cult, she served General Ketheric Thorm and followed the deity of death – Myrkul. Because of that, Radija looked down on other cultists, especially the True Souls, who were infected with altered illithid tadpoles. Trusted servant of Ketheric Thorm's, Radija resided within the Moonrise Towers and stood guard of the towers' rooftops – the area off-limit to all. When the battle started between the Absolutists of the towers and the group of heroes who opposed them, aided by Harpers, Radija was presumed slain in the struggle shortly before the adventurers confronted and ended the unlife of General Ketheric Thorm.

Randelo questioned the nobleman Arclath Delcastle and the coinlass Amarune Whitewave in the aftermath of the murder near her home, one morn in Mirtul of the . Luckily, despite his suspicions, a watchman had been following them all night, and vouched for the fact they had taken no part in the murder.

## Raereene

Raereene was a War Wizard of Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Raereene was gorgeous, with midnight-blue hair and large, dark eyes.

Personality
She was used to being the focus of men's attractions, and not impressed by it.

Abilities
Raereene could teleport other people under her own power, at least the distance between Castle Obarskyr and Stormserpent Towers, both in Suzail.

Possessions
Raereene had access to a highly destructive scepter of sorts, capable of blasting a beholder into smithereens; it was almost certainly a blast scepter of sorts.

Activities
She was a War Wizard, working out of Castle Obarskyr.

Relationships
Raereene had an tryst with a young palace server, Kreane; the two employed the Room of the Fire Wyrm for amorous activities while in Castle Obarskyr. In time for the Council of the Dragon, Raereene had become an ally of the late Alusair Obarskyr and Elminster Aumar.

History
Arclath Delcastle bumped into Raereene when he was seeking Glathra Barcantle's office at Castle Obarskyr in the lead to the Council of the Dragon of . Later, when Raereene was enjoying a tryst with Kreane at the Room of the Fire Wyrm, she was briefly spied upon by Alusair Obarskyr and Elminster Aumar, who were in a secret passage with peepholes into the room.

Later, Alusair sought her out when needing to teleport Elminster into Stormserpent Towers; she was not initially inclined to help, but Vangerdahast's vestige then tried to convince her, and Raereene agreed to do it. Raereene also later annihilated one of the vampire Manshoon's mind-controlled bodies, that of a beholder, when it was attacking Arclath and the dancer Amarune Whitewave in the palace. She continued to help Alusair and Elminster's schemes afterwards, being noted as the one vigilant person by Storm Silverhand.

One early evening after the Open Feast of, while dancing at the Dragonriders Club, Amarune Whitewave overheard Raoryndar or Rindlar or something, who had been an honest enough patron for three years, bring up the rampage three nobles had earlier gone on. Amarune quickly decided to hop off, get her clothes on, and go tell Arclath Delcastle about it.

## Rake

A rake was a common farming implement found in Faerûn.

Description
Rakes were often made entirely out of wood, but more expensive or magically-imbued ones were usually fitted with metal heads. The handle was usually 3–5 feet (0.9–1.5 meters) long, and there were anywhere between two and twenty stiff or flexible prongs. The rake used its many-pronged head to sweep and scoop up loose materials, such as hay.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered typical rakes with heads made from twigs for 2 sp, and exclusive ones with expensive Chultan bamboo heads, which were long and strong enough to gather up to  of material at a time.

## Raging Tears

The Raging Tears was the personal caravel of Urdogen the Red, the infamous pirate. It became a cursed spectral ghost ship bound to the Domains of Dread.

History
Urdogen's violent raiding led to a a great naval battle on Eleint 11, 1209 DR, when the navies of Cormyr, Impiltur, Aglarond, Sembia, and the Vilhon Reach united to end his threat. Eventually losing the battle, Urgoden fled while raging against the gods and swearing eternal vengeance on his enemies. A strange mist rose about the ship, transporting it, the crew, and Urdogen to the Domains of Dread. Urdogen transformed into a specter and then shared his curse with the crew, becoming one of the Domains' lesser Dark Lords as he terrorised its inhabitants. The ship regularly returned to the Inner Sea on foggy, moonless nights, and predated upon any ships they could catch, only to vanish before dawnbreak.

## Raylen Jannath

Lord Raylen Jannath was a member of the Parliament of Peers from the noble patriar Jannath family of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
By the, Lord Raylen Jannath was already a member of the Parliament of Peers. When the Absolute crisis arrived to the Gate, Raylen was noted by the Cult of Bane as one of the eight top patriars of the Parliament, vulnerable to bribery, blackmail, or direct threats. Raylen Jannath was a prime target for the cult's extortion thanks to the Jannath family's profitable mining operations. The Banites planned on exposing Lord Jannath's affair with a "rent goblin" in the Little Calimshan neighborhood of the Gate. The plan included a rumormongering publication of Baldur's Mouth from under Ettvard Needle pen that would include details of the affair that would be meaningful to the patriar, following by an anonymous note left in his bedroom.

Shortly before the armies of the the Absolute attacked the Gate, Lord Raylen Jannath voted in support of crowning lowborn Lord Enver Gortash as the city's Archduke. Raylen was present for Gortash's coronation that took place in the audience hall of Wyrm's Rock. Following the coronation, he proudly proclaimed his support for Gortash' accession, stating the new Archduke was a strong leader and the Gate needed a political shake-up. In a conversation with Lord Myer Ravenshade, Raylen voiced his approval of the steel watchers as they provided protection for the trade routes of the Gante and not the Baldurians.

## Rauntlavon

Rauntlavon was an apprentice mage to Iyriklaunavan in the mid–8 century DR.

Description
Rauntlavon was young, and almost certainly a teenager.

Personality
Feeling attracted to Ladylord Nuressa, he wished he was older, stronger, and better-looking than what he was. As such, he had a certain admiration for his master Iyriklaunavan's elven grace and gruff manner.

Abilities
His skills at magic were enough to help steady his master's spells.

Relationships
He studied sorcery under Iyriklaunavan, whom he admired.

History
During the chaos of the Art in, Rauntlavon helped his master steady a farscrying spell; the result was only catastrophic enough to scare the Ladylord Nuressa, one of the four Lords of the Castle, into coming. There was much talk, and Rauntlavon learned that Ladylord Nuressa had been a slave, and that the four Lords of the Castle had found the deed to it once one of them slept in a shrine to Mystra after gambling away all their coin and trying to steal some of it back. More importantly, he learned that his master had seen Azuth battling off mages and staring at a ghostly sorceress trying to kill a Chosen of Mystra who was known to the Four Lords of the Castle, for they'd disturbed the tomb where he'd been held in stasis. He was also made aware that if he blabbed about any of those things he'd need to find a new home. In turn, he told the two Lords that the castle had been built with magic, and would be in danger if the chaos of the Art grew any more dire.

Description
Randall was a tall ivory-skinned aasimar man with pupil-less eyes and a glowing mark on his forehead. He wore a brown leather trenchcoat and a wide-brimmed hat, both of strange design unseen on Toril.

History
In the 15 century DR, Randall Lyric followed a group of adventurers from the world of Eberron to the village of Eveningstar in Cormyr on Toril during their quest to investigate the nearby ruins of the Haunted Halls, and to the Ball and Chain tavern outside of the Wheloon Prison. He followed the adventurers to other worlds, including to Saltmarsh on the world of Oerth, Sharn on Eberron, the Feywild, Barovia in the Domains of Dread, and the legendary planar-traveling Isle of Dread.

## Raumark

Raumark was an Archwizard of Hlondath, who led the southern migration that founded Halruaa.

Description
The man's appearance was only known to Elminster Aumar, and even then, through spell-shared memories not his own.

Abilities
He could cast a spell that created a whorl of disintegrating energy.

History
Raumark had grown dissatisfied with the attitude of his fellow arcanists in Netheril, and planned on an exodus even before the Fall.

In the, shortly after the Fall of Netheril, Raumark was involved in the founding of Hlondath, where he hoped to make a stand against the phaerimm.

Rather than stay, however, he led a great flight of skyships, including not only fellow arcanists but also their households, and so a majority of Low Netherese, south to a secluded, broad valley that would later become Halruaa. Deliberately trying to reduce the influence of fallen Netheril, he learned the language of the locals, rather than speak Netherese. He also prepared his people for a fight with the pursuing phaerimm he expected.

In a vision granted to him by Mystra in, Elminster learned that Raumark had been in a hall of white pillars, where he cast and then jumped into a spell-created whorl of disintegration.

Legacy
The library of Raumark was named after him.

## Rainstorm

Rainstorm was a divine evocation spell granted to druidss and rangers by their deities.

Effects
The rainstorm spell conjured forth a  square area of strong magical rain within their eyesight. The rain damaged all creatures of cold or fiery nature as long as the spell lasted. Additionally, all creatures within the rainstorm area had a cointoss of a chance of being struck by a lightning every six seconds they spent within the area of the spell's effect. All flaming spells, such as flame blade or fire shroud, and fire elemental effects, such as the blazing aura of a salamander, were immediately extinguished. The spell lasted for twelve seconds.

## Ravu Jhuristhen

Ravu Jhuristhen, better known by his chosen name, Oblivion, was a powerful and fearsome werewolf High Priest of Malar the Blood God from the city of Luskan in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Oblivion was a hulking but lean square-jawed human of  tall with a shaved head and wild black beard. He had piercing yellow wolf's eyes that were permanently changed by lycanthropy.

In his wolf or his favorite hybrid form, his massive body was covered in pitch-black fur.

Personality
Ravu possessed wild, animalistic impulses that scared most people around him. In order to hide his nature, he learned to hide these impulses behind a stern mask of stark emotional withdrawal. That mask was dropped once battle ensued, transforming his face into a terrifying grimace of the savage glee of bloodlust. Open worshipers and preachers of other religions were always his first targets, with tearing their heads and ripping their bodies apart being Oblivion's pleasure.

Abilities
When in battle, the priest's savage nature led him to fight in melée most of the time, using a magical +2 scythe when in his humanoid shape. Because of that, he used spells that boosted his strength and protection, such as greater magic weapon, endurance, righteous might, and divine favor.

Possessions
As a High Priest, Ravu wore a set of mithral breastplate armor decorated with Malar's unholy symbol, hidden under baggy robes when not in a temple of his savage god. He carried potions of haste and potions of cure moderate wounds and wore a brooch of shielding upon his cloak of charisma.

Activities
In his position of leadership, Oblivion acquired a small army of agents across the city that kept him well informed on the enemies of the church, and the priest himself regularly used scrying.

He often ventured into Luskan's markets and town squares, preaching the greatness of his deity and demanding tithes.

Lycanthope members of Oblivion's flock were known to hunt the homeless for sport.

History
Ravu Jhuristhen was born in the northern city of Luskan to a family of candlemakers. He was a vicious child, bigger, stronger, and smarter than his peers, who became a bully and leader of a gang by the age of nine winters old. By the age of twelve, Ravu's parents demanded he end his unsavory lifestyle and learn the trade of candle making; in response, their son robbed and burned the shop down.

This act attracted the attention of numerous criminal organizations in Luskan; however, the Church of Malar had one thing to offer to Ravu—lycanthropy. The young criminal knew that lycanthropy meant power and accepted the church's invitation without hesitation. By the age of sixteen, Ravu had massacred two of the other young initiates, by the age of sixteen, six. Soon, Ravu changed his name to Oblivion, a name that meant he would be bringing oblivion to the enemies of Malar. By the age of twenty-six winters, Oblivion had reached the rank of a High Priest, earning fear and respect from his peers and lessers.

## Ravid

Ravids were long, one-armed, serpentine creatures native to the Positive Energy plane.Monstrous Compendium Planescape Appendix III. Edited by Michele Carter and Ray Vallese. (TSR, Inc.), pp. 88-89. ISBN 0-7869-0751-7.

Description
A ravid was a solitary creature that looked like a long albino eel or snake 7 feet (2.1 meters) long, except they had a single claw jutting out from their body near the head. Weighing around 75 pounds (34 kilograms), they floated by some innate power.

Behavior
Surprisingly docile, they would float around the Positive energy plane and, on encountering any inanimate object, would imbue them with life. These animated objects would attempt to protect the ravid to the best of their abilities.

Abilities
Ravids were infused with the positive energy of their native plane. As such, they would defend with rending claws against anyone attacking them or, if the hostile creature was undead, lash out with their tails and hurt them with jolts of positive energy.

This positive energy also enabled the ravid to animate objects, which they did continually, amassing a small following of animated objects that followed them around.

Should they lose their ability to float by some magical means, they became greatly hampered as their fighting was predicated on their flight.

Appendix

References

---

## Rast

Rasts were creatures living on several distant planes, most notably the Elemental Plane of Fire and the Quasi-Elemental Plane of Ash.

Description
A rast was a creature of ash and cinder with ten to fifteen legs, a bulbous floating body, and great head filled with teeth. It was the size of a great dog and weighed around 200 pounds (90 kilograms).

Behavior
They roamed around in swarms of half a dozen and continuously consumed any prey they were able to catch and kill.

Abilities
Rasts were brutal cunning creatures able to paralyze their foes with but a glance. They would attack with four of their many claws, grapple their prey, and drain its blood, all while it was still alive.

Were they lose to their ability to float by some magical means however, they would become greatly hampered as their fighting was predicated on their flight and they could to little else than try to regain their bearings.

Appendix

## Ravens Bluff/The Shutters

The Shutters was a neighborhood mostly comprised of private and secure warehouses in the Crow's End District of Ravens Bluff, located across the Fire River from Shadystreets.

Description
The Shutters was a relatively small neighborhood of about eleven uneven city blocks with a single bridge across the Fire River connecting it to Shadystreets. The Ravensbridge laid on the northeastern end of the neighborhood on Stonekeep Concourse. Two streets that ran from east to west formed the neighborhood's southerns border: Hammerstone Street to the east and Red Sails Way to the west. Karen Court ran parallel these two streets, while Mraedara's Street, Stonekeep Concourse, Burning Shield Lane, Landleen Lane, Helhavryn's Walk, Castles Way, and Deepaxe Way connected them, crossed, and moved further south, beyond the neighborhood's border.

The neighborhood was sparsely populated with most inhabitants being mercenary garrisons who ensured the tightly-shuttered warehouses of the Shutters remained unburglarized. The neighborhood's shores were muddy, allowing the locals fish for river eels as well as other edible sea creatures. Abundant seagulls were often knocked out with stones and sent straight to the locals' stewpots. Most animals found here were just food, apart from ravens, who remained unharmed. These reasons were why The Shutters were mostly ignored by tourists and visitors.

Notable Locations
 Ambra's Small Boats Rental & Harbor Rescue/Retrieval, a riverside shop, north of Deepaxe Way's end, run by Ambra, a retired adventurer along with a crew of seven hin in the late 14 century DR.
 Delmer's Ferry on Deepaxe Way's offered a rowboat passage across the Fire River, run by Wamaer Delmer in the late 14 century DR.
 Fellowship of Exalted Adventurers, a young boys' club formed in the late 14 century DR.
 Fish Tails, a fried fishcake factory two doors west from The Last Jack.
 Fruits & More, a grocer located next-door to Muldryn's Chainworks on Karen Court. It specialized in sugars, jams, and compotes, as well as crude berry-infused cordials.
 The Last Jack, a quiet inn with a safe taproom located on the riverside where Stonekeep Concourse and Mraedara's Street crossed.
 Muldryn's Chainworks, a chain-forging workshop at the very end of Karen Court, run by Anskaler Muldryn in the late 14 century DR.

## Ravens Bluff/Pumpside

Pumpside was a neighborhood in the Southside District of Ravens Bluff, located across the Fire River, south of The Shutters and east of the Tlasbras Neighborhood.

Description
The neighborhood of Pumpside was a startling contrast of poverty and wealth that laid in the shadow of the House of War's high walls. Most of the building south of Stonekeep End were rundown rooming houses protruding from the wood bordering the neighborhood.

Geography
Pumpside was a square neighborhood of around fifteen city blocks. To the east, Pumpside's border was Mraedara's Street, to the west – Zeldazar Street, Hammerstone Street – north, and Stonekeep End – south. Stonekeep Concourse, Burning Shield Lane, Landleen Lane, and Helhavryn's Walk, all ran parallel to Zeldazar Street and Mraedara's Street. In turn, Conant Court and Kalikoolan Lane were laid perpendicular to them. Samarglast Lane cut the neighborhood diagonally, from where Stonekeep Concourse crossed with Hammerstone Street and ended at a moot with Stonekeep End and Zeldazar Street.

Notable Locations
Brothers Galgolar Pawnshop, south of the crossing of Hammerstone Street and Stonekeep Concourse, run by Jeffers Galgolar and Malachi Galgolar in the late 14 century DR.
Cliff's Bard & Swill on Stonekeep End and Landleen Lane, a late night drinking establishment and a festhall that became popular in the late 14 century DR.
House of War, a temple complex dedicated to Tempus that occupied a quarter of the neighborhood.
House of Whelk, a busy Merchant House on Helhavryn's Walk and Samarglast Lane, that produced fish pastes and fishing nets.
Magestrand Manor, a manse on Zeldazar Street and Samarglast Lane, home to Oracle, a one time Minister of Divination of Ravens Bluff.
Ravens Bluff Sanitation Facility that replaced a smelly junkyard on Zeldazar Street and the western end of Hammerstone Street.

History
It was forgotten, why the neighborhood was called Pumpside. However, most historians thought that the name originated from a horse-powered water pump used to irrigate the fields that once grew where the Pumpside neighborhood would eventually grow.

In the, after the conclusion of the war against Myrkyssa Jelan, a family man carpenter was discovered to had served under the Warlord's banner during the war. Despite being a law-abiding citizen since the war's end, he was lynched in Pumpside.

In the, City Watch officers were investigated over bribery and racketeering.

## Ramarthant Horaerays

Ramarthant Horaerays was a mage of the late 15 century DR and the Magister during the turn of the century.

Personality
He was quiet and thoughtful.

Abilities
Ramarthant had mastered his trove of enchanted devices sufficiently to become a powerhouse of magic. One unique spell of his allowed him to transport beholders from elsewhere to other places as he pleased.

Possessions
Ramarthant had acquired a significant cache of Netherese enchanted devices.

History
Before becoming the Magister, Ramarthant had been an adventurer for three decades, through which he had amassed a small hoard of enchanted items. In the, with Mystra's blessing, Azuth offered the office of Magister to Ramarthant Horaerays upon the death of Valargrim Hond.

Upon receiving the mantle of office, he was immediately set upon by sixteen liches sent by Szass Tam, and defeated all of them. He then retaliated by magically sending several Zhentarim beholders straight into Thaymount, where they caused great chaos. No further attempts from Szass Tam were known.

He since disappeared into a number of lairs across Faerûn. Of note, he'd defeated a young arcanist from Thultanthar who'd tried to seize the Magister's cache to grow mightier.

Activities
Ramarthant had not faced many challenges, because he was difficult to find. He spent most of his time modifying magic and devices he owned, to better defend himself with.

Appendix
References
