## Ravens Bluff/Tent City

Tent City, also known as Tent Town, was a sprawling tent community outside of the North Gate of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR. It was considered by some to be an unofficial district of the city.

Description
The community's name derived from it being largely made up of canvas or brightly-dyed silk tents. It was also host to corrals, stalls, and a few permanent structures. Many of the tents had wooden plank walkways between them.

The Cross Road, the road connecting Tantras and Mossbridges to Ravens Bluff's North Gate, cut right through the center of Tent City. A smaller, less impressive gate was situated on the road to mark the outer boundary of Tent City.

Trade
Tent City was host to a variety of different merchants, vendors, and ware hawkers. Additionally, there was always something of a hiring fair going on, though it was typically avoided by those who wished to hire discreetly, as City Watch officers and informants were frequently keeping tabs to crack down on any slave-dealings.

History
In the spring of,No firm date is present in any of the versions of At Last, Ravens Bluff. The ages of certain NPCs in Counterfeit Dreams, which is set a few weeks later, agree with sourcebooks such as Gateway to Ravens Bluff, the Living City (which is set in 1355 DR) and Inside Ravens Bluff, the Living City (which can also be surmised to be set in 1355 DR). For more information, see here. the notorious con artist illusionist Mortimer Mittlemer was running a scam outside Tent City's gate with his wife Marilyn and associate Elmerth Willowit, impersonating a City Guardsmen and shaking down newcomers for 10 gold pieces in return for "permits" to enter Ravens Bluff, as well as smaller fees for keeping their weapons or horses.

When The Three Women War ravaged the Vast countryside, those who had the money to do so moved into Ravens Bluff, hoping to wait out the conflict. Those less fortunate remained outside the city's walls, where they suffered through illnesses and lack of food. Eventually bands of thieves moved into Tent City and began using it as a staging ground for their raids on Ravens Bluff. Out of concern for the city proper's safety, Tent City was demolished and its inhabitants dispersed.

The many vendors that once did business in Tent City were sent overflowing into both the Market District and the Torchtown neighborhood of the Uptown District, seizing vacant rooms in many of the city's abandoned buildings. The residents with money filled Ravens Bluff's one-story cottages, causing such overcrowding that they were eventually torn down in favor of constructing taller structures. Others found homes in the rundown buildings along the docks and the banks of Fire River. The more destitute of the displaced had little choice but to sleep in the city's alleys or upon its rooftops.

Many felt that Tent City would never return, with the Three Womens War having demonstrated the superior safety of life behind Ravens Bluff's walls. During this period of absence the space was at one point used by the city to erect tents for the Champion Games. Eventually, however, through monetary donations and charitable manual labor by Ravens Bluff's adventuring community Tent City was reconstructed. Residents slowly trickled back in following its reconstruction, with many who were well off before the War having fallen into poverty. Some looked to the city for work, while others left to pursue their own adventuring career, and some became so desperate that they joined a charismatic figure's Banite cult.

Notable Locations
 The A.V.O.W. shelter, a permanent structure and animal shelter that was established after the Three Women War with the hope of finding homes for all the animals that lost their owners during the conflict.
 The Dancing Bear inn.
 Marigold's Menagerie, a permanent structure animal shelter and animal training center. It managed to remain standing following the forced dismantling of Tent City, due in part to it being co-opted by a band of thieves.

Inhabitants
Tent City was a tight-knit community whose population fluctuated with the seasons, usually peaking during the summer months. While many of its residents were destitute families and children, with many beggars working the crowds that passed through.

Residents were suspicious of non-residents, though not hostile or rude. People simply kept to themselves, unless something threatened them directly, most of the time unwilling to offer up information freely.

Notable Inhabitants
 Amy-Sue, a destitute child.
 Madame Zorbano, a fortune teller, lived there in a tent of fine purple silk that was festooned with silvery moons and stars and topped with a yellow pennant.
 Mather Sagra, a man that was skilled in providing people disguises.
 Tent town was among the places that the roving street vendor Friar Cookpot frequented.

## Ramora's Guile Shipping and Freight

Ramora's Guile Shipping and Freight was a shipping business in the Lower Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Ramora's Guile Shipping and Freight was a modest one-story building with a basement.

Services
Ramora's Guile Shipping and Freight was known for its selective business practices. It rarely accepted freight from walk-in customers, consistently claiming full capacity. It was in fact a front for small-time smuggling operations, which specialized in attaching contraband to the undersides of vessels using watertight containers. Smuggled goods were securely stored on-site, protected by four trained war dogs and a series of magical and mechanical traps.

As of 1370 DR, Ramora's Guile focused primarily on transporting smokepowder for the Brigadier.

Inhabitants
Ramora's Guile Shipping and Freight was owned by Sheila Kells. Despite numerous attempts by the Pirate Tyrant, Misker, to take over her operations, Sheila had thus far managed to resist them, though many believed it was only a matter of time before she was forced to align with his organization.

In support of their arrangement, the Brigadier provided Sheila with an enchanted dagger for personal defense and a small enchanted crystal globe, which she wore on a chain around her neck. Should the globe be shattered, it signaled the Brigadier, who would then arrive fully armed to defend his assets at Ramora's.

Appendix
References

## Raven's Eye

The Raven's Eye, formerly known as the Red Sail Inn or Red Sail Tavern, was a tavern and inn in the hamlet of Crow's Foot in the 14 century DR.

Location
The Raven's Eye was situated close to Ravens Bluff's protective outer stone wall. The back of the building faced an alley, where trash was strategically piled to ensure it was little trafficked.

Structure
The tavern was housed in a stout wooden building that stood two stories tall and had a basement. The stairs to the second floor, which boasted sixteen rooms, were along the western wall of the building. In addition to the front entrance, the building had an exit in its office and kitchen, the latter of which was aligned with the stairs to the basement.

Atmosphere
The business primarily catered to sailors and rogues. It was considered rather standard as far as taverns went around Ravens bluff, but was always full of customers. Patrons could often be found playing card games or darts.

Activities
Under its third owner the Raven's Eye was used as the center for a fencing and smuggling operation, a forgery racket, as well as a safehouse for wanted criminals. The criminals were mostly confined to the inn's basement in order to avoid detection, whilst its wanted "guests". Those wishing to conduct illicit business with the owner usually entered from the back entrance.

Inhabitants
The tavern was first owned and operated by Gov Landrum, who originally bought it with the aim of helping others with their problems. By 1360 DR, the tavern was sold to an old fisherman and later Verity Overinn.The Perils of Politics makes reference to the events of the module Counterfeit Dreams, stating they took place five years ago. That module has been estimated to take place in 1355 DR, based on the ages given therein of Lord Charles Blacktree and Vernon Condor.

Verity employed a number of tavern wenches, all attractive and buxom, as well as a watchmen who stood guard on the second floor, watching out from a boarded-up window.

History
On a foggy night around the spring or fall season of the, a group of human hooligans posing as members of the Night Watch were trying to press a protection racket on Landrum, demanding he pay them 40 silver pieces. Luckily for Landrum, actual members of the Night Watch arrived on the scene alongside vampire hunter Jack Harker and chased off the imposters.

In the, a group of fire worshipers emerged in Crow's FootInferno in the Living City makes some references to the events of Nightwatch in the Living City, referring to them as having happened four years prior. As that module has been estimated to be set in 1355 DR, that would place the events of Inferno in 1359 DR. called the Cult of Red Ruin and established a temple close to the tavern. Its members frequented the Red Sail Inn quite often, giving Landrum the impression that their cult was an excuse to party.

Sometime later that year the cult found and trapped within an iron flask a pyre elemental. Over the next two weeks the cultists caused several fires in Ravens Bluff, until one day they caused a significant wave of fires in Crow's Foot, Old Town, and the Warehouse District. A group of volunteer members of the city's firefighting service, the Red Ravens, later stopped by the Red Sail Inn on the night of the fires just as four members of the Cult of Red Ruin were stopping by to pick up eight bottles of wine for their evening services. The cultists would leave the establishment at around 11 and a half bells.

In 1360 DR, Gov Landrum suddenly had to leave Crow's Foot in a hurry and sold the Red Sail Inn to an old fisherman who renamed it "The Bloated Crab". Business floundered under the fisherman and it was not long before Verity Overinn convinced him to sell the building to her. She would go on to change the name to "The Raven's Eye" and start using it as a base for various criminal operations.

## Rags Deelarma

Rags Deelarma was a beggear and a refugee from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Deelarma was a human woman with a light skin tone and blue eyes. She kept her brown hair cut short.

History
In the, an army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in the Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees in the process along the way. Rags Deelarma was one of the refugees forced from her home. She found shelter in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate at Nortale's Hostel. To survive, she begged for coin in front of the Counting House. One of the bank's wealthy clients, Vrindula Mardassus, liked to flaunt her money in front of Deelarma, counting coin but not showing sympathy to the beggars.

## Rajiram

Baron Rajiram  was a cloud giant smiling one and pirate captain of the Sword Coast the late 15 century DR. Following the breaking of the Ordning in the 1480s DR, he sought the Ark of the Mountains.

Description
Rajiram was smaller than most cloud giants. He covered his features with a bifurcated red mask - one half depicting a smile, the other half a scowl.

Personality

Rajiram was particularly greedy and was also quite intelligent.

Abilities
Rajiram was a powerful spellcaster. As a cloud giant, he could cast spells such as gaseous form and control weather. As a bard, he had access to spells like major image and tongues. He also had the ability to change his shape and assume the form of beasts or humanoids that he had encountered previously.
Possessions
Rajiram's pirate ship was Thunderbound, a flying galleon with multiple decks. . Rajiram carried a staff of thunder and lightning. He used the staff to control the weather around Thunderbound.

Rajiram sometimes used a simulacrum of himself to take his place. The simulacrum could communicate telepathically with Rajiram.

Activities
So that he could improve his position in the Ordning, Rajiram pillaged treasure and magic items.
In the late 15 century DR, Rajiram conducted most of his piracy along the Sword Coast and on the Sea of Swords.
Relationships
Rajiram's crew included many humans from the Nelanther Isles.
Also serving in his crew were minotaurs, goblins, and oni.

Among the notable members of his crew were Boff Badcrack the hill giant Mouth of Grolantor and Sláturhaust the storm giant quintessent. He was also served by Lord Dworkin, a cloud giant archmage.

Rajiram's followers were completely devoted to him; they feared his anger more than they feared death. He gave frost brand scimitars to the pirates he liked the most.

History
Rajiram was exiled from his home, and so came to the Nelanther Isles. There, he won favor with the pirates that lived there and rose to become a powerful pirate captain himself.

The Baron was active for at least decades before the 1480s DR, when he clashed with SEER and Bitter-root of the Lords' Alliance.

In the 1480s DR, Rajiram attacked Durlag's Tower, in an attempt to recover a magical tablet which was able to reactivate the Ark of the Mountains. SEER sent adventuers to combat the Baron's forces and obtain the treasure of the Tower before he could. Rajiram hired twin mercenary dwarves, Bahldrak and Bellegia Slagspit to patrol the surrounds of the Tower and assasinate the adventuerers. Later, as the adventurers entered into the tower, Rajiram sent his simulacrum to obtain the tablet. The adventurers countered Rajiram's efforts and recovered the tablet.

Later, Rajiram sent his followers from his flying ship to plunder the ruins of the Ulcaster School of Wizardry near Beregost. SEER and the recently-revived Ulcaster, sent adventurers to protect Ulcaster's secret library from the pirates.

Still desperate to recover the tablet that had once been in Durlag's Tower, Rajiram sent a message to Beregost. Unless the tablet was given to him, he threatened to violently destroy Beregost from Thunderbound. In order to save the town, SEER gathered adventurers together and raised the Ark of the Mountains from Durlag's Tower. The adventurers took control of the Ark, and flew it in an epic sky battle against the Baron.

## Ramathanhur

Ramathanhur was a son of the god Anhur who warred against Red Wizards in the 10 century DR. After his troop of soldiers were defeated, he became sealed in the Darkcrypt.

Description
Ramathanhur appeared as a nearly  tall Mulan human male. He had a hooked nose and shining blue eyes. His skin was gold in color, and appeared to glow softly. He had no hair anywhere on his body.

Personality
Ramathanhur was charismatic and clearly regal. He was particularly knowledgable about religious matters. He preferred to talk rather than fight.

Abilities
Ramathanhur had access to a variety of powerful abilities. He could not be harmed by acid, cold, disease, or electricity.
He could replicate the effects of the spell light any time he chose, and Three times per day he could replicate the effects of the spells holy aura and protection from evil. He could also replicate the effects of the spells aid, bless, cure serious wounds, detect evil, hallow, holy smite, holy word, neutralize poison, remove disease, and symbol. He could channel Anhur's power to increase his power in battle. He could also smite those who did not follow Anhur and heal those by touching their body.

He was extremely skilled in fighting with a khopesh.

Possessions
Ramathanhur used a pair of gloves of lightning, as well as a holy keen khopesh, a lion's shield and enchanted scale mail.

Activities
In the Darkcrypt, Ramathanhur regularly spent time honing his fighting ability.

History
In the, Ramathanhur was a commander in the armies of Mulhorand. He was stationed on the plateau of Priador when the Red Wizards rebelled against Mulhorand. His forces lost the battle and were pushed back to Thazalhar. In order to atone for his military failure, Ramathanhur elected to remain at the Darkcrypt, a disused memorial to Re. He made an oath to protect the Darkcrypt for the rest of his life, or until Mulhorand reclaimed its Thazalhar.

To this end, a high priest of Horus-Re sealed him in with a forbiddance spell, and collapsed a portion of cliff onto the shrine. Along with Ramathanhur was a collection of valuables taken during battles with Red Wizards. The high priest was slain shortly afterwards by a force of Red Wizards.

Some time after the Time of Troubles of the, a spectral mage named Kethoth Uuthrakt discovered the location of the Darkcrypt using spell scroll of locate object. Kethoth had been on the side of the Red Wizards in 922 DR, and some of the treasures buried with Ramathanhur were his. Seeking to reclaim his posessions, he animated skeletons and sent them to tunnel into Darkcrypt. Ramathanhur slew many of the skeletons himself.

Rumors & Legends
All who knew that Ramathanhur guarded the Darkcrypt were slain shortly after he took up his oath. Because of this, none knew what had happened to him or that he remained buried beneath the earth.

## Ramen Byrrd

Raman Byrrd was a human wizard and brother of Reman Byrrd, living in Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR.

Description
Raman was  tall and weighted . He had willowy blond hair and blue-green eyes. He was near indistinguishable from his twin brother Reman, and most found it very difficult to tell the two apart, as even their speech patterns were similar.

Abilities
Raman and his twin brother Reman shared an uncanny empathetic link. The twins were able to share ideas and thoughts between them without uttering a single word.

He was capable of casting the spells alarm, alter self, audible glamer, change self, clairaudience, continual light, detect evil, detect magic, feather fall, fireball, flaming sphere, gust of wind, hold person, hypnotism, invisibility, know alignment, magic missile, mirror image, spider climb and stinking cloud.

Possessions
Raman typically wore dark brown and blue clothing, to contrast with the pale colored clothes that his brother preferred. Though sometimes he swapped outfits with his brother to confuse others.

On his person, Raman Byrrd wore his magical bracers of defense and was armed with an enchanted dagger. The brothers also owned a spelljammer ship.

Relationships
He was very close with his twin brother, and the two would sometime imitate each other to confuse outsiders.

History
The most notable event in Reman and Ramen's lives was their father's mysterious disappearance that was punctuated by the transfer of ownership of a spelljammer ship. Sceptical of their father's claim of a flying ship they were shocked to receive it upon a desolate mountain peak, not wishing to sell the last item their father purred the family fortune into, they decided instead to turn the magical vessel into an amusement ride.

## Raelus Ustarren

Raelus Ustarren was a Zhentarim agent in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
To Storm Silverhand he was competent at being a killer and a thief.

Relationships
He was a Zhentarim.

History
Raelus Ustarren's look was known by Storm Silverhand from her time scrying on Manshoon.

In, Raelus was one of the men at Warmfires House for the murder of Maervidal Iloster, a scrivener. After he escaped, the Zhentarim operative Calivar Murpeth interrogated the local wench Talatha, actually Storm Silverhand in spell-guise, to find him. She claimed that she'd helped Maervidal escape because he had four armed men with him, and they threatened her; when asked to point them out, she singled out Worvor Drezil, Strabbin Stillcorn, Rungo Baerlan, and Raelus Ustarren. One of them tried to fight, but Taerach Thone slew him with a dagger to the neck on the spot. He slew the second one before they could respond, and then slew the other two despite putting up  a fight.

## Razagnol

Razagnol was a powerful glabrezu demon accidentally summoned in the 15 century DR.

History

When the Shadovar of Thultanthar reassembled the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll at Granwell Keep, one of the attendant arcanists fell to temptation and intuitively attempted to manipulate its magical powers. Unfortunately for all involved, this resulted in a portal to the Abyss opening in the Keep, letting Razagnol step through. He gloated for a moment at the terrified Primes, then ushered in a horde of dretches and fire reavers that rained from the sky around him. Despite the local apocalypse and Razagnol's own power, the ultimate victors were the forces of the Harpers, who defeated the Shadovar and banished Razagnol. They were then joined by Rina Belostos and Elminster, who took the Nether Scroll away for safekeeping.

## Ravens Bluff Hall of Records

The Ravens Bluff Hall of Records, also known as the Town Archives, was the central municipal archive of the city of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR, until it had burned down in 1371 DR.

Location
The Hall of Records stood on the southwest corner of the intersection of Raphiel Road & Turnhelm Street, in the Gowntown neighborhood of Ravens Bluff's Temple District.

Structure
The building was a long hall, with walls lined in documents, and a cellar.

Activities
This building was entrusted with the storage and protection of all manner of Ravens Bluff's civic documents, including permits, deeds, the records of Ravenian knighthoods, what scarce records were coerced out of the guilds of Ravens Bluff, and some historic books. Though not all of the city government's plans were stored within the hall, as many forms of documentation were kept in the Raven Museum and customs records were kept in Narwhal Manor.

Citizenry could enter the Hall of Records at any time to request the aid of its clerks in finding some form of documentation, though they would not freely give out details on just any Ravenian's file out of concerns that doing so may violate their privacy.

History
Around the, sometime after the Three Women War, a group of adventurers entered the Hall of Records on an idle day and inquired with a male clerk that was on duty for a list of Ravenians with permits to own mammoths, as they were on a search for the missing pet Billie. The clerk responded by asking them for a name, explaining that their records for animal permits were organized by the names of permit holders. Pulling out a large tome, he flipped to a section labeled "MO" and gave them the ringmaster Jack Mooney as an example, citing his permits for seven mammoths in addition to permits for carnivorous apes, lions, tigers, and bears. The clerk was at a loss without a name, offering to look through his books for them in his spare time, but such a task would take a month. Before leaving they inquired about a mysterious Mistress Charnela, to which the clerk informed them she had a resident alien permit valid for a tenday period.

Sometime later on a late night, a large fire broke out in the Hall of Records. The building was completely destroyed by the fire before the Red Ravens could control it, burning many civic documents considered to be irreplaceable, including the trade agreement with The Murkos and records and documents created during Belanor Fenmarel's time as the Acting Mayor of Ravens Bluff. The latter made up the largest portion of documents to be destroyed, leading to rumors that government officials loyal to Belanor may have been responsible for the blaze. A number of documents, namely the records of knightly orders and Ravenian guilds, managed to survive due to copies having been stored in the Raven Museum in case of just such a blaze.

While the Clerical Circle and Knights Council quickly reacted to the loss of the Hall of Records, the Ravenian government had no immediate plan of action to address the catastrophe. Of the two the Knights Council's reaction was the most swift and pronounced, promptly  printing fliers requesting all knights to notify the Council of their allegiances by providing copies of their official heraldry and their official certificate of knighthood. In the wake of this it came to light that there were many bogus knights in Ravens Bluff with no proper claim to their title. Additionally, the Knights Council made staunch vow that from then on the records for all knightly orders were going to be safely kept within the Hall of the Raven, even if the Hall of Records was rebuilt. The Clerical Circle, meanwhile, appointed a representative to investigate the fire and hopefully uncover its cause. Their chosen representative was a Kossuthan priest, whom prior to the incident had been working alongside a building inspector to aid in the demolition of buildings that were deemed condemned by the city.

Within that same year efforts were underway to reconstruct the building, but slowed by the intervening interests of various merchants, who viewed the plot of land where the Hall of Records stood as a prime location for a luxury apartment block. Reconstruction was also complicated by the burnt ruins and their scorched cellars being frequented homeless squatters, whom the City Watch frequently had to chase off the premises. Beyond the homeless, the burnt husk of the Hall of Records was reported by the Watch to be a frequent site for clandestine meetings between lovers, merchants making private deals, and adventurers involved in shadowy acts of intrigue.

Inhabitants
The clerks of the Hall of Records included Trudy and the Deneirian Caitlin.

## Raztan

Raztan was an armsman of Athalantar during the 3 century DR.

Activities
Raztan was an armsman.

History
In the, Raztan was in the force holding Narthil when Elmara, a priestess of Mystra who was secretly the Prince Elminster Aumar came there. After she frightened a number of armsmen with an illusion, a Magelord of Athalantar arrived. The magelord approached her at Myrkiel's Rest, an inn where she was eating, along with a number of armsmen, including Raztan, one whose name the magelord knew. He ordered Raztan to sword her; though Raztan did, she was under the effects of an ironguard spell, and all Raztan accomplished was hurling himself face-first into a bowl of soup. After she countered them by casting a spell that appeared to turn their swords to snakes, Raztan and the armsmen fled.

## Ravens Bluff waterfall

The Ravens Bluff waterfall was a geological feature among the Vastian city's hills that was considered unique for it being upwelling – its waters emerged from a fissure in the ground, coming off of an underground stream. The exact origins of the fall's water was unknown to most Ravenians.

Description
These roaring falls fell off a cliff at a height of  and seemingly vanishing into the ground. At some point in the late 14 century DR, the top of falls expanded into a body of water known as Lake Eternal.Polyhedron #110's article "You Are Here -----> X" (1995) reconnected the existence of a lake at the top of this waterfall. No such lake is mentioned in the adventures Tempest in a Teapot (1994) and Attention Shoppers (1996), despite the latter stating that it "utilizes all of the new city maps found in Polyhedron issue 110." The former maintained the description from Gateway to Ravens Bluff, saying the falls "burst forth from a fissure in the ground". However, the presence of this lake was later repeated in the maps of Ravens Bluff included in The City of Ravens Bluff (1998) and the Forgotten Realms Interactive Atlas (1999), therefore its existence cannot easily be written off.

Roughly  above the base of the falls, situated behind the waterfall itself, was a ledge running along the cliff face that protruded out . Leading from the middle of this ledge was an opening in the cliff face,Tempest in a Teapot provides two separate heights for the waterfall, giving it as "200 ft" on page 9 and "300 ft" on page 10. Pages 4 and 9 state of the module state that its cave is roughly at the bottom of the waterfall. Page 10 gives contradictory placements of "30 feet [from the bottom]" and "200 feet from the top [of the falls]", with the former height being given again on page 11. The later published Attention Shoppers refers to the cliff as being "200 ft" above the ground and its cave as "200 ft below". Taken all together, the first height seems to be the predominant one. a crevice measuring  high and  wide. The narrow, mist choked passage, opened into a cave that housed a one-way portal to the Abyss. On all but the night of a new moon, only tanar'ri could enter this portal.

The rocky surface at its bottom, seen by very few Ravenians, was exceedingly wet, slippery and covered in both algae and a constant thick mist. This mist billowed upwards, thinning out at  above the ground, though on some days it could rise as high as, to the very top of the cliff. The bottom of the falls were aptly named Bottomless Lake, as its bottom was not easily visible. In reality its depth was no more than, but beneath it lay a portal to the Elemental Plane of Water.

Geography
This waterfall was situated at a cliff known as Lemming's Leap, among the hills east of the Uptown District neighborhoods Mortonbrace and Skymbles, behind the city's coliseum.The location of the waterfall first mentioned in Gateway to Ravens Bluff, the Living City was never explicitly identified, however, its description matches that of the waterfall depicted in that sourcebook's postermap.

History
At some point in the 14 century DR, a bard attempted to explore the cave behind the falls and therein met his demise. The bard left behind a mist tent, hovering invisibly  above the cave's entrance, and it would remain there for several years. Several years later, a nabassu moved into the cave and over the next year would go on a discrete murder spree in Ravens Bluff. Feeling its time on Prime Material was finished and anxious to return home to the Abyss, the nabassu decided to set about getting its hands on the Holy Container of Wisdom, a lost Tempuran relic that it had learned the whereabouts of. It believed this sacred silver teapot be nothing more than a variant iron flask, a magical item that would be a great boon to its campaigns of terror. Ever cautious, the nabassu sent out his ghast minions to retrieve the object, disguising both them and itself with dust of illusion to appear as no more than human brigands.

That day a group of wandering adventurers witnessed Sir FM Lillywhite, an elven rogue paid and coerced by the nabassu to masquerade as a noble, being accosted by the faux brigands. The disguised nabassu than fled from the scene holding a reproduction of the teapot. When questioned as to what had occurred, FM Lillywhite explained to the adventurers that the teapot contained his soul and implored them to retrieve it. The adventurers chased after the nabassu, who was leading them to the ruins of a small tower within the city walls, wherein the real Holy Container of Wisdom was being guarded by Field Surgeon Kantor and three warriors of Tempus. Rather than engaging them in combat, the adventurers explained why they wanted the teapot. Kantor then agreed to go with the adventurers to confront FM Lillywhite and his heretical claims of the Holy Container housing his soul, bringing along with him the relic after having cast weighty chest upon it.This adventure could diverge into two paths, with one having the adventurers murder Kantor and the warriors of Tempus. As Kantor was later established in Attention Shoppers to be at the final battle, the path where the adventurers cooperate with the Tempurans is thus considered the canon path.

This confrontation went down at the Trende Cafe, where a feigning illness Lillywhite tried to offer all manner of valuables in exchange for the teapot – initially 60 platinum pieces and six air spores, then eventually throwing in an everbountiful soup kettle and jar of preserving out of desperation. Meanwhile, Kantor was fuming and outraged, cursing Lillywhite and trying to provoke him into a fight. The Field Surgeon's volatile behavior in such a public venue resulted in the adventurers having to restrain him. Sargent Katon "Bloodletter" Krag then burst into the restaurant with a booming voice, even more frothing furious then Kantor had been, casting prayer and demanding to know what was going on. Kantor tried to quickly explain, but Krag resorted to casting zone of truth. Sources differ on whether Lillywhite managed to sneak away with the real teapot or was publicly stripped and chained up by Krag. In the former case, Krag shouted at the adventurers to recover the Holy Container of Wisdom or face death. In the latter case, Krag asked them to help in verifying rumors of a tanar'ri in the area. Regardless of whichever way it went down, sources agree that Krag then lead the party to the Ravens Bluff waterfall.

Over the deafening roar of the falls, Krag shouted, "Trust in Tempus, just leap over the edge here, and he will guide you to your destiny!", having foreknowledge of a hidden ledge. Field Surgeon Kantor and Rictor of the Cracked Tusk leapt first, then followed by Rictor's friends Long Time Running and Niblik, and then the adventurer Cateyes was forcefully shoved off the cliff – later recounts of this story by the Tempurans would say they were pushed "by the holy word of Tempus". Down they went, at last confronting the horrid nabassu and its ghast victims. These events were later commemorated on the cliffs atop the falls with a special, albeit small, plague.

Around the, during the Three Women War, the magics of the Ministry of Art somehow interfered with the portal at the base of the waterfall.

## Rattlesnake

A rattlesnake was a common variety of viper and poisonous snake, named for a keratin segment at the end of its tail, that produced a loud rattling or buzzing noise when vibrated, to ward off predators.

Description
A rattlesnake could grow to be  in length. They had a distinct rectangular pattern to their skin. The rattle at the end of their tails was similar to that of a kamatlan.

Behavior
The disposition of these snakes were once equated by the wizard Elminster with that of a cockatrice. When surprised, they were known to raise their heads and coil them slightly into an 's' shape to prepare for striking.

Abilities
The venom of a rattlesnake was less dangerous than that of some other snakes. It affected the nervous system, causing a paralysis in the breathing muscles that could lead to death by asphyxiation. The herb jalap protected against their bites and the bent priests of the Azuposi were notably immune to their venom.

Ecology
Habitats
Rattlesnakes were typically found in dry and rocky terrains. In Faerûn, they could be found in the land surrounding the Battle of Bones battlefield, as well as in its mountain passes, in parts of the Serpent Hills, and in Emory Wood.

Beyond Faerûn, rattlesnakes could be found in the bleak deserts of Maztica, such as the Pasocada Basin, as well as possibly in Zakhara.Caravans states that the character of Khafaz ibn Dahz enjoyed telling tall tales, with one example given being, "ghostly rattlesnakes that attack unsuspecting travelers in their sleep". However, this is not to say for certain if these snakes can be found in Zakhara.
Usages
These creatures were hunted by some, both for consumption and for the use of their snakeskin goods such as boots. The pelt of a rattlesnake was often worth around 1 to 4 gold pieces. Their rattles could be used as part of traps, tied to ropes to act an alarm whenever a snare was triggered. In Maztica, their rattles were harvested for use as a material component in the spell snake charm.
Relationships
Rattlesnakes were symbols of the deities Sseth and Zaltec, who was sometimes described as having two fangs like those of a rattlesnake. And these creatures were among the commonly chosen totem animals for a totemic druid.

Trivia
 The god Sseth infrequently manifested himself as the sound of a rattlesnake's rattle to warn his priests of danger, particularly imminent ambushes. Additionally, the third highest rank within his hierarchal church had the title "Rattler".
 Rattlesnakes were among the creatures that a psionicist could emulate with the power animal affinity.
 Some serpentfolk were found to develop rattles on their tails similar to those of rattlesnakes.

## Ramparts of Night

The Ramparts of Night was a massive abyss in the Earthroot domain of the Underdark.

Description
The abyss did not reach the surface, but it plummeted an immense distance before connecting to the Shadowfell. That is, it was effectively bottomless; falling into the abyss would lead to a great canyon on the Shadowfell.

Geography
The abyss was located underneath the Sunrise Mountains, the mountain range forming the eastern border of Thay.

History
During the Spellplague, the borders of the Ramparts expanded to encompass a far greater region, reaching an area underneath the Copper Mountains of Murghôm.

Inhabitants
The walls of the Ramparts of Night were inhabited by cloakers and other creatures of shadow, including shadar-kai from Netheril. A large enclave of gulgars also made their home in the walls, and tribes of gulgars living on the surface in the Sunrise Mountains were descended from there.

Notable Locations
The shadar-kai settlement of Ikemmu was located on the southern edge of the Ramparts.

## Rand (druid)

Rand was a druid and sometime mercenary in Cormyr in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Rand was an elderly halfling man with a thick white beard. He wore an outfit of hides and furs.

Personality
Rand was stubborn, grumpy, and rather unimpressed by those around him.

Abilities

Rand was a druid of some power, able to cast call lightning, creeping doom, enveloping swarm, and ice storm.

Relationships
Rand didn't care for Dom and his goals at all, only focusing on finding his lost nephew Stiev.

History

The old druid spent much time trying to find his nephew Stiev, and was manipulated into joining Dom's mercenary force thanks to the promises of Dom helping find him. This arrangement came to an end when Risen Netheril hired Dom to seize a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll from the Harpers, attacking Bensen Virandson as he transported it down Greenweald Lane. Though he took part in the battle, Rand refused to fight to the death for a cause he didn't care for, and simply surrendered to let the Harpers through.

## Ravel Puzzlewell

Ravel Puzzlewell was a night hag and the greatest of the Gray Sisters of the Wastes.

Description

Ravel was exceptionally hideous, even for a hag. Her dark-purple skin was covered in warts, scabs, and hairy moles. A blackish tongue lay hidden behind purple lips and yellowed tusks. A few long wisps of black hair hung from her spotted scalp, matching a single furry brow. Her ember eyes were bloodshot with black veins.

Despite a stick-thin, near-starved frame, she possessed a sagging pot belly. Typically hunched over, she moved with a pained skitter.

She was clad in a tattered dress made of white, gummy cobwebs.

Abilities

Personality

Possessions
Before she was exiled by the Lady of Pain, Ravel possessed dozens of magical items such as a charm of blackness, a cube of force, the fiendish eye of Kalem'Darr, a harp of pandemonium, and a ring of human influence.

History
The Nameless one used Kesai-Serris' blood as a portal key to enter the Black-Barbed Maze.

Fragments of Ravel
Ravel was a branching being whose life was like a massive tree extending across the planes. Due to her strange nature, Ravel existed in Sigil and other places simultaneously in multiple forms as different people. The Nameless One encountered several of these manifestations during his journey. Following the hag's death, these fragments of the witch began to unravel, resulting in their lives ending.

 Ei-Vene, a tiefling who used her talon-like fingers to embalm and stitch up bodies for the Dustmen in Mortuary.
 Marta, a seamstress who worked in the Buried Village
 Mebbeth,
 Aphril, a seer and an inmate of the Spellhold asylum in 1369 DR.Aphril makes comments about happenings of these otherwordly planes and places that are clearly references to other Dungeons & Dragons video games, such as Neverwinter Nights and Planescape: Torment. For Neverwinter Nights, she commented: "I see... a place... coming soon... where there is... never winter... where people have control... over their own stories." For Planescape: Torment, which was set in the center of the multiverse, Sigil, the seer commented: "I see... a city of changing streets under the Lady of Pain. My eyes... pluck out my eyes.", a reference to the ruler of Sigil. She also made a comment about the Nameless One, the game's protagonist: "I see... a walking corpse... he speaks... to a pillar of skulls? See, it is madness!"
 Firtha Kerdos, the elderly lady with cats in Targos
 The Gloomfrost Seer,

Appendix
Notes

Behind the Scenes
 In Planescape: Torment and Planescape: Torment: Enhanced Edition, Ravel Puzzlewell is voiced by Flo Di Re.

Trivia
 Planescape: Unraveled

 Chris Avellone cited Ravel as a direct influence on the creation of the Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic II: The Sith Lords character, Kreia. He had narrative ideas intended for Ravel that went unexplored, and he channeled them into the character of Kreia.

## Raurivyl Ornshield

Raurivyl Ornshield was a former slave turned pirate and the captain of the infamous Scimitar of Fire, which was active on the Sea of Swords in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was an overweight Calishite human who kept his curly beard perfumed.

Abilities
Despite his weight, he was known to be a brawler.

Activities
Captain Raurivyl Ornshield was feared and respected as a pirate. With a hot-tempered hatred for the realm of Amn, Raurivyl focused his attacks on Amnian vessels, such as coasters and caravels. His ship's flame-red sails were infamous, scaring many merchants as soon as they set eyes on them, and his ship's swiftness and his crew's skill meant few other vessels could outdistance or outmaneuver the Scimitar of Flame and no ship he targeted got away unscathed.

At the least, Raurivyl would seize a defeated ship's cargo and capture important persons as hostages or hold them for ransom—if none was paid, the unfortunate captive was keelhauled. Those who survived were marooned on a shore miles from the nearest port, giving the Scimitar more time to get away. His attacks rarely killed a whole crew of scuttled a ship but he nonetheless took great joy in sinking Amnian ships.

Relationships
Captain Raurivyl Ornshield was served by his first mate, the half-orc Dreak. They made an effective pair, with Raurivyl having the brains and plans and Dreak the brawn.

Most of his crew were unaware of their captain's reasons for targeting Amn, let alone his past.

History
Although Calishite, Raurivyl Ornshield was born in Waterdeep around 1338 DR. While still a child, he was kidnapped and sold into slavery in the subterranean city of Skullport, and was transported to Amn. There, from the age of eight, he grew up a slave on a plantation lying east of Lake Esmel that was owned by the Colwyvv family. He befriended a fellow slave, the half-orc Dreak.

Then, with the advent of the First Dragonspear War in the, the number of guards at the plantation were reduced. Seizing the advantage, Raurivyl, Dreak, and other slaves made their escape. However, instead of running to safe havens in the north or south, Raurivyl and Dreak went west, taking a ship and fighting a vengeful war against Amn, its merchant houses, and the Council of Six. Helming the Scimitar of Fire, they became infamous as pirates, feared by both Amnian and Calishite ship captains for over a decade by 1370 DR.

Some time in the 1360s DR, a Waterdhavian sailor named Faerim came to be a captive aboard the Scimitar of Fire. Serving as a crewman, he would be a witness to Raurivyl's voyages to the west.

Seeking a desert island he might use as a safe haven and base, Raurivyl took the Scimitar of Fire westward on several voyages beyond the Gull Rocks. However, they found naught but a vast expanse of empty ocean. On three occasions they encountered the naval vessels of Evermeet, which rose out of the sea, hovered in the air, and tilted to direct their strange weaponry at the Scimitar. Via magical speaking horns, the elves ordered the pirates to leave. Sensibly, Captain Ornshield obeyed, but still the flying elven ships followed them until they were long gone.

Following months of such futile searching, Raurivyl returned to the Nelanther Isles. There, Faerim escaped, even taking some pirate treasure with him back to Waterdeep, along with tales of the elven ships. Raurivyl sheltered the Scimitar in a cavern harbor on the island of Carcathen and later located a safehouse he wasn't yet sure about in Ilyaport in Lantan.

As of 1370 DR, after laying low for a number of months at Carcathen, both Raurivyl and the crew had grown restless. Fortunately, the turmoil they found in Amn at the time attracted their attention. Yet Captain Raurivyl Ornshield attracted another's attention too: the Rundeen mercantile organization, who desired the Scimitar of Fire's services as pirates in the Shining Sea. In trade, they offered safe havens, ship repairs, and whatever else Raurivyl might wish for. However, although intrigued by a profitable alliance, he knew the Rundeen acted much the same as his Amnian enemies and so he was just as likely, if not more so, to start targeting Rundeen shipping instead.

Despite this, the Scimitar of Fire was reported to be Rundeen pirate vessel later that year.

## Ralahn el Matyr

Pasha Ralahn el Matyr was the guildmaster of the Jhasinnar Sodality in the city of Calimport, Calimshan, in the late 14 century DR.

Activities
Based at The Sultry Siren, a Sodality-controlled festhall in the city's Emerald Ward circa 1370 DR, Ralahn covertly used the establishment for trading goods on the black market and fencing stolen jewelry. He also allowed pashas and sultans a discreet escape from their enemies, or just embarrassment, via the secret passages in the cellars, so they owed Ralahn and the Sodality a favor for their lives or their reputations.

History
In the, the Trades Conflagration blazed across northern and central Calimport. The Sultry Siren was damaged, but calling in some favors, Ralahn had it repaired in less than a month.

## Ratin Sesescor

Ratin Sesescor was a cheese-loving rat that dwelt in Alexander Rainforest's home in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
Before his death, Alexander Rainforest tried to befriend Ratin Sesescor, feeding him various cheeses, earning him the name "cheese-dropper, a person who drops cheese." Alexander discovered that the rat liked red, white, yellow, mellow, and sharp cheeses crumbled into small bits, and disliked smoked, blue, and waxed. When Alexander was murdered by the serial killer Dolor, Ratin eagerly ate the "red morsels" spilled from his friend's body, enjoying them more than cheese. Ratin did not speak Common, on account of being a simple rodent, but he witnessed the murder and could describe the killer as a dwarf dressed in all red, to whoever was capable of communicating with a beast through magic.
